📊 Secure Living Marketing Dashboard  -  Complete Build Specification (Final + Pro Tips)

1. Objective
To build a single Looker Studio dashboard that provides a complete view of marketing performance, from initial search to signed agreement, segmented by User Type (Tenant/Landlord).

2. Core Principle
Every metric, chart, and filter must respect the User Type segmentation. This is the primary dimension for analysis.

3. Data Sources & Connections
 Google Analytics 4 (GA4):
 sessions (filtered by medium = organic)
 event_count (for the whatsapp_chat_click event)
 Google Search Console (GSC):
 For impressions, clicks, CTR.
 Blended with GA4 for consistent landing page reporting.
 Google Sheets (CRM/WhatsApp data):
 Weekly CSV uploads until API automation.
 Schema:
| Lead ID | Date | User Type (Tenant/Landlord) | Lead Status | Signed Agreement (TRUE/FALSE) | Messages Count |

4. Global Dashboard Controls
 Date Range Selector
 User Type Filter (Tenant, Landlord, All)

5. Calculated Fields (Looker Studio)
Organic Conversion Rate (OCR):
COUNT(CASE WHEN event_name = "whatsapp_chat_click" THEN 1 END) /
COUNT(CASE WHEN session_medium = "organic" THEN 1 END)
Lead Quality Score (LQS):
(
  COUNT(CASE WHEN Lead Status = "Qualified - High" THEN 1 END) +
  COUNT(CASE WHEN Lead Status = "Tour Booked" THEN 1 END)
) /
(
  -- This counts all rows in the current filter context
  COUNT(CASE WHEN TRUE THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
  - COUNT(CASE WHEN Lead Status = "Not a Lead" THEN 1 END)
)
Final Conversion Rate:
COUNT(Signed Agreement) / COUNT(Organic Sessions)

6. Panel Build-Out
Panel 1: SEO Performance
 Metrics: Organic Sessions, OCR, CTR (from GSC), Keywords in Top 10/100.
 Visuals:
 OCR Trend (Tenant vs. Landlord lines)
 Landing Page Table (URL, Sessions, OCR, CTR)
 Keyword Coverage Bar Chart

Panel 2: WhatsApp Engagement
 Metrics: Total Leads, LQS, Signed Agreements, Avg. Messages/Lead.
 Visuals:
 Scorecards (Total Leads, Signed Agreements, Avg. Msg/Lead)
 LQS by User Type (bar chart)
 Funnel: Leads --> Qualified Leads --> Signed Agreements

Panel 3: Acquisition Funnel
 Stages:
 Organic Sessions (GA4)
 WhatsApp Leads (Sheets)
 Qualified Leads =
 COUNT(Lead ID)
 WHERE Lead Status IN ("Qualified - High", "Tour Booked")
 Signed Agreements (Sheets, TRUE only if contract logged in CRM)
 Visualization: Funnel chart (User Type filterable).
🔑 Pro Tip: Blending Strategy
Developer should build this funnel using a blended data source (GA4 + Sheets) with Landing Page + Date as the join keys. This ensures Signed Agreements can be correctly calculated as a % of Organic Sessions.

Panel 4: Retention & Success (Phase 2)
 Metrics (future): Churn Rate, Community Engagement Index (CEI), Net Promoter Score (NPS).
 Visuals (placeholder): Scorecards for Churn, CEI, NPS.

7. Developer Pro Tips (for Seamless Execution)
 Looker Studio Syntax
 Use exact field names from the connectors (e.g., event_name, session_medium).
 Don't rely on pseudocode field names in the spec.
 User Type in GA4 (Phase 2 Enhancement)
 Best practice: push User Type as a custom dimension when a user clicks WhatsApp.
 This will allow Panel 1 (SEO) to segment tenants vs. landlords natively in GA4.
 For Phase 1, continue using the Google Sheets User Type filter.
 Signed Agreement Definition
 TRUE only when a fully executed digital contract is logged in the CRM.
 Prevents confusion with verbal or pending deals.
 Formula Transparency
 In LQS, add developer comment lines to explain:
 Numerator = Qualified Leads only
 Denominator = All leads except "Not a Lead"

8. Implementation Checklist
 Connect GA4 to Looker Studio.
 Connect GSC and blend with GA4 by Page URL.
 Set up Google Sheet with schema.
 Connect Google Sheet as source.
 Create calculated fields (OCR, LQS, Final Conversion Rate).
 Build global filters (Date, User Type).
 Build Panel 1 visuals.
 Build Panel 2 visuals.
 Build Panel 3 funnel with blended data source.
 Add Panel 4 placeholders.
 Test segmentation (Tenant vs. Landlord).
 Validate formulas against sample data.

FB: Purchase (with value) 
Google Ads: SignedAgreement

4. Parameter Consistency
 user_type = Tenant or Landlord.
 Defined once in data layer and passed with every event.
 Example (WhatsApp click):
dataLayer.push({
  event: "whatsapp_chat_click",
  user_type: "Tenant"
});
➡ Developer ensures it persists across the session.

5. Value Capture (CRM Integration)
 For signed_agreement (Purchase), dynamically pull contract value from CRM/webhook instead of using static numbers.
 Example for Meta Pixel:
fbq('track', 'Purchase', {
  value: 85000,
  currency: 'KES',
  user_type: 'Landlord'
});
 This enables true ROI tracking in Ads.

6. Naming Convention
 Adopt snake_case for all custom events:
 ✅ whatsapp_chat_click, tour_booked
 ❌ WhatsAppClick, SignedAgreement
 Document conventions in a shared Tracking Schema Sheet for consistency across devs/analysts.

7. Privacy & Compliance
 Integrate a Consent Management Platform (CMP) into GTM.
 Only fire tags after consent (Marketing, Analytics).
 Update Privacy Policy and cookie banner to mention cross-platform tracking.

8. Testing & QA
 Tools:
 Meta Pixel Helper --> check events + parameters.
 Tag Assistant --> confirm Google Ads + GA4 firing.
 GA4 DebugView --> confirm user_type parameter.
 Test scenarios: Tenant WhatsApp click, Landlord consultation booking, Signed agreement.

9. Retargeting & Insights
 Facebook Custom Audiences:
 Tenants who clicked WhatsApp but didn't book a tour.
 Landlords who booked consultation but didn't sign.
 Google Remarketing Lists:
 Organic Tenant visitors with no WhatsApp click.
 Landlords with WhatsApp click but no agreement.
 Looker Studio Dashboards:
 Every chart segmented by user_type.
 Funnel panels show conversion path differences.

🚀 Strategic Fit into Secure Living Master Plan
 Central Nervous System --> This tracking setup feeds both marketing campaigns and your analytics dashboard.
 Cost per Acquisition (CPA) --> With values from CRM, you'll know the exact ROI per Tenant vs. Landlord.
 Smart Retargeting --> Enables nurturing flows ("Landlords who started onboarding but didn't finish").
 Scalable Growth --> With naming conventions + sGTM plan, future complexity is manageable.

👉 Why: Option A = features. Option B = benefits. Test to see which resonates most.

"4.9/5 Stars from 347 Tenant Reviews"

 Dynamic Scarcity (Recommended):
 Pull real-time (or simulated) data:
 Units available --> "✅ 1 Unit Left at This Price"
 Tour count --> "⚡ 5 Tours Booked Today"
 If not possible immediately, start with static text but set rules to update often.
👉 Why: Fake scarcity is dangerous. Dynamic data builds trust.

 Mobile:
Floating WhatsApp button + "Chat to Book"
👉 Why: Clean, fast path to conversion.

 Name
 Phone

👉 Why: Segmentation feeds your analytics + CRM.

 Mini dashboard mockup (occupancy %, rent collected, etc.)

👉 Why: Landlords buy into control + data clarity.

7. Post-Conversion Flow

"Thanks for your interest in the 2-bedroom apartment in Nairobi! What is the best time for a tour?"

 Immediate SMS/email confirmation.
👉 Why: Removes uncertainty, boosts trust, and prevents lead drop-off.

Analysis & Integration: Building a Data-Driven Marketing Machine for Secure Living
This isn't just tools thrown together  --  it's a working system where every step of the customer journey is logged, measured, and optimized.

1. Connecting the Funnel to Our Content
Front-End (Landing Pages + Ads)
 Facebook/Instagram Ads --> Landing Page (headline/image A/B tested) --> CTA ("Browse Properties")
Back-End (Tracking + CRM Integration)
 Awareness --> Interest: Visitor clicks CTA --> inquiry via WhatsApp.
 Logged automatically in CRM (HubSpot/Zoho).
 Assign Lead Quality Score.
 Consideration: Tenant books meeting (Calendly).
 Synced into CRM, linked to lead.
 Decision: Signed agreement (Zoho Sign).
 Status updated: Lead --> Customer.
 Loyalty: Referral tracked back to the CRM.
 Attribute referral to original acquisition channel.
👉 This ensures every action from ad click to signed agreement is captured and attributed.

2. KPIs in Secure Living Context
 Organic Conversion Rate
 % of Google visitors who act (WhatsApp, call, form).
 Tells us if SEO & website are generating real leads.
 Lead Quality Score
 Prioritizes leads based on seriousness.
 Example:
 +1 = specific property inquiry
 +2 = moving in within 1 month
 +3 = asks about lease terms
 High score = sales-ready, improves ad targeting.
 Net Promoter Score (NPS)
 "How likely are you to recommend Secure Living (0 - 10)?"
 Directly predicts referrals --> cheaper growth.
 Conversion via WhatsApp
 Deals closed from WhatsApp inquiries.
 Validates WhatsApp as a primary acquisition channel.
 Authority Measurement (SEO)
 Track keywords: "apartments for rent Nairobi," "secure apartments Kilimani," "property management Kenya."
 Climbing Google = sustainable growth.

3. Actionable To-Do List
🔧 Step 1: Install & Configure Tracking
 Google Analytics 4 (GA4) + Search Console --> must be live on the website.
 UTM Parameters --> unique links for each campaign (FB vs Instagram vs WhatsApp).
 Result: You'll know exactly which ad brought the conversion.
🔧 Step 2: Choose and Set Up CRM
 Start with HubSpot CRM (Free):
 Create custom fields: Lead Source, Lead Score.
 Require all client interactions (WhatsApp, calls, meetings) to be logged.
🔧 Step 3: Design Your First A/B Test
 Test: Headline on tenant page.
 Tool: Google Optimize (until sunset), ConvertKit free plan, or Unbounce (low cost).
 Metric: GA4 tracks clicks on "Browse Properties" or WhatsApp button.
🔧 Step 4: Launch NPS Survey
 2 months after move-in --> Send Google Form by email/WhatsApp.
 Q1: "How likely are you to recommend us?"
 Q2: "Why did you give that score?"
 Result: Measure service quality --> feed back into improvements.

Secure Living  -  Funnel & KPI Implementation Guide
This guide ensures your marketing funnel is fully trackable, automated, and data-driven.

1. Tracking Setup (Foundation)
 Google Analytics 4 (GA4):
 Install GA4 tracking code on all website pages.
 Set up Events for:
 Click "WhatsApp" button
 Click "Call" button
 Form submissions
 Google Search Console:
 Verify website.
 Submit sitemap for SEO monitoring.
 UTM Links:
 Use Campaign URL Builder for each ad campaign (FB/Instagram/Twitter).
 Example: ?utm_source=facebook&utm_medium=ad&utm_campaign=tenant_leads

2. CRM Setup (Lead Management)
 Choose HubSpot CRM (Free) or Zoho CRM (Free for 3 users).
 Create custom properties:
 Lead Source (Facebook Ad, Organic SEO, Referral, WhatsApp, Call).
 Lead Score (rule-based: +1 property inquiry, +2 move-in urgency, +3 lease terms).
 Train staff: every WhatsApp, call, or meeting must be logged in CRM.

3. WhatsApp Integration (Interest Stage)
 Install WAHA API (Docker) or Evolution API (Node.js) on your server.
 Connect API --> CRM:
 Every WhatsApp inquiry creates/updates a lead.
 Add "Lead Score" automatically based on keywords (e.g., "lease terms" = +3).
 Track "Conversion via WhatsApp" in CRM + GA4.

4. Meetings & Decision Stage
 Calendly (free) integration:
 Sync Calendly with CRM (meeting = lead update).
 Zoho Sign (free tier) or DocuSign trial:
 Store signed agreements in CRM.
 Mark lead as Customer upon signature.

5. Loyalty & NPS Tracking
 Referral Tracking:
 Add field in CRM: "Referred By."
 Log referrals back to original source.
 NPS Survey:
 After 2 months --> Trigger Google Form/Typeform via WhatsApp or Email.
 Q1: "How likely to recommend (0 - 10)?"
 Q2: "Reason for score?"
 Store NPS results in CRM for long-term loyalty tracking.

 KPI 2  -  Lead Quality Score: CRM (average lead score per source).
 KPI 3  -  Net Promoter Score (NPS): Google Form/CRM survey results.
 KPI 4  -  Conversion via WhatsApp: CRM + GA4 event tracking.
 KPI 5  -  Authority Measurement (SEO): GA4 Search Console keywords + Ahrefs Webmaster Tools (free).

✅ End Result: Secure Living transforms into a data-driven growth engine where every ad, click, chat, and referral is tracked --> optimized --> scaled.




Hack 1: The "Dark Funnel" Tracking  -  Capturing the Unseeable
Goal: To accurately attribute conversions that happen offline or through untraceable word-of-mouth, revealing the true ROI of your brand-building campaigns.
Execution Plan:
 Create Campaign-Specific Codes & URLs:
 For every major campaign (e.g., a Facebook Ad campaign for Kilimani apartments, a Google Ad campaign for landlord services), generate a unique promo code (e.g., KILIMANI24) and a vanity URL (e.g., secureliving.com/kilimani24).
 The vanity URL simply redirects to your main listings page but is easily trackable.
 Incentivize Code Usage:
 In your ad creative, include the offer: "Mention code KILIMANI24 when you inquire to receive a [500 KES airtime credit / free cleaning service]." The incentive must be low-cost but high-perceived value.
 Train Your Sales/Support Team:
 This is the critical step. Implement a strict script or mandatory field in the CRM for every new lead that comes via phone or WhatsApp:
 Agent: "Hello, welcome to Secure Living! Before we begin, could you please tell me where you heard about us today? Did you happen to see a promo code anywhere?"
 The agent then selects the source (e.g., "Facebook Ad - Kilimani") and enters the code (KILIMANI24) in a custom field in the lead record in HubSpot.
 Analyze and Attribute:
 Now, you can run a report in your CRM to see:
 Which promo codes are driving the most leads.
 The true Cost Per Lead for your Facebook Ads, even for leads that didn't click.
 This data allows you to defund campaigns that generate "likes" but not conversations, and double down on the ones that truly build brand recall and offline conversions.

Hack 2: The "Loyalty Loop"  -  Systematizing Word-of-Mouth
Goal: To transform satisfied customers into a proactive, paid sales force, drastically reducing customer acquisition cost (CAC).
Execution Plan:
 Build a Simple Referral Program Page:
 Create a page: secureliving.com/refer
 The page should clearly state the rewards for both parties (e.g., "You get 10% off rent, your friend gets 5% off their first month").
 Use a tool like ReferralRock or GrowSurf. These tools integrate with your CRM and automatically generate unique referral links for each user.
 Integrate into the Customer Journey:
 Tenants: 30 days after a successful move-in, an automated WhatsApp message is sent: "Loving your new home? Refer a friend and earn your next month at a discount! Here's your personal link: [unique-link]"
 Landlords: After their first successful rent collection, an email is sent: "Experience hassle-free income? Refer a fellow landlord and we'll waive our next management fee. Share your link: [unique-link]"
 Track and Fulfill Automatically:
 The referral tool tracks clicks and sign-ups from each unique link.
 When a referred friend becomes a customer, the tool automatically triggers a reward fulfillment process (e.g., generates a discount code for the referrer, alerts your team to waive a fee).
 This creates a self-sustaining growth loop.

Hack 3: The "Competitor Conquesting" Hack  -  Poaching High-Intent Leads
Goal: To directly target users who are already in the market and considering your competitors, capturing demand you didn't create.
Execution Plan:
 LinkedIn Ads for Landlord Poaching:
 Targeting: Use LinkedIn's targeting options:
 Company Name: Target employees of competing property management firms (e.g., "Knight Frank," "Hass Consult").
 Job Title: "Owner," "Director," "CEO," "Landlord," "Real Estate Investor."
 Ad Creative: The ad copy must be disruptive: "Tired of hidden fees from [Competitor Name]? Secure Living offers full transparency. See our pricing model."
 Google RLSA (Remarketing Lists for Search Ads):
 Step 1: Create a "Remarketing Audience" in Google Ads of all users who visited your site but did not submit a lead form or sign up.
 Step 2: Create a new search ad campaign targeting this specific audience.
 Step 3: For this campaign only, bid on your competitors' brand keywords: [competitor name], [competitor name] reviews, [competitor name] pricing.
 Result: Your ads will appear at the top of the search results when someone who already knows you Googles your competitor. This is the ultimate intent-capture hack.

Hack 4: The "Content Gateway" Hack  -  Building Authority
Goal: To become the thought leader in your space, generating high-quality backlinks for SEO and capturing expert-level leads.
Execution Plan:
 The Linkable Asset: "Nairobi Rental Market Report"
 Process: Use your own data (anonymized and aggregated) on rental prices, occupancy rates, and tenant preferences across suburbs. Combine this with publicly available data.
 Design: Produce a beautifully designed, PDF report.
 Promotion: Email this report to journalists at Business Daily, TechCabal, and property blogs. Offer them an exclusive angle or quote. The goal is for them to write an article about the report and link back to you as the source. This one report can generate dozens of powerful .ke backlinks.
 The Gated Content: "The Ultimate Landlord's Guide to Data Security"
 Content: This is a deep, technical guide that speaks directly to the pain points of high-value landlords. It explains SOC2 principles in simple terms, data privacy laws in Kenya, and how Secure Living protects tenant and landlord data.
 Gate it: Place this guide behind a form on a landing page. The form captures: Name, Email, Number of Properties.
Lead Nurturing: Anyone who downloads this is a high-intent, quality lead. They are automatically tagged in your CRM as "High-Value - Security Conscious" and enter a dedicated email nurture sequence about your premium,

Section 17: Service Provider & Professional Onboarding

🎯 Purpose
Secure Living relies on two key operational groups:
1️⃣ Professionals  -  Internal or external individuals who represent Secure Living for due diligence, verification, inspections, and transaction oversight.
2️⃣ Service Providers  -  Plumbers, electricians, cleaners, landscapers, valuers, etc., who handle maintenance, repairs, or specialized work.
Without a strong onboarding system, trust breaks down. This section covers:
 ✅ Vetting process
 ✅ Training & certification
 ✅ Onboarding workflows
 ✅ Ongoing performance management
 ✅ How Professionals & providers integrate into the Secure Living dashboard

🏗 1. Professional Types & Service Provider Roles
🔹 Professionals (Secure Living "eyes and ears")
 Internal Staff Professionals:
Full-time Secure Living employees (trusted for sensitive or high-value work).
 External Verified Professionals:
Vetted third-party contractors (e.g., valuers, surveyors, lawyers) working under Secure Living's framework.
✅ Examples of Professional Tasks:
 Title deed verification
 Site visits
 Construction progress checks
 Tenant interviews

🔹 Service Providers (Hands-on workers)
 Plumbers, electricians, masons, roofers
 Cleaners, landscapers, pest control
 Stagers & furnishing specialists
✅ Examples of Service Provider Tasks:
 Repair broken taps or pipes
 Conduct pest fumigation
 Install new fittings
 Stage an apartment for Airbnb

📝 2. Vetting & Verification
Secure Living cannot afford rogue Professionals or artisans. The onboarding process is rigorous.
✅ Vetting Steps
1️⃣ Application Form (Online)
 Basic personal info (name, phone, email)
 ID upload (Kenyan ID or Passport)
 KRA PIN
2️⃣ Background Check
 For Professionals: Verify education (e.g., surveyor certificate)
 For providers: Check certifications (e.g., electrician license)
3️⃣ Police Clearance Certificate (Good Conduct)
 Required for all Professionals and high-contact service providers.
4️⃣ Work Samples & References
 For artisans: Photos of past work
 For Professionals: Previous inspection or due diligence experience

🎓 3. Training & Certification
🔹 Professionals
 Orientation Module: Introduction to Secure Living's values (Integrity, Transparency, Client Trust)
 Digital Dashboard Training: How to:
 Log tasks
 Upload reports/photos
 Request approvals
 Compliance Training: KRA verification, GIS tools, fraud spotting
🔹 Service Providers
 Customer Etiquette 101:
How to interact with tenants/landlords professionally.
 Repair Reporting Training:
How to submit before/after photos to trigger payment.
 Payment System Overview:
Understand how they're paid after Professional approval.
✅ Certification:
After completing training, Professionals and providers receive Secure Living ID & digital badge in the system.

📋 4. Onboarding Workflow
flowchart TD
    A[Applicant Fills Online Form] --> B[Document Upload & KYC]
    B --> C[Background & Police Check]
    C --> D[Training Module Completion]
    D --> E[Admin Approval]
    E --> F[Activated in System Dashboard]
    F --> G[Assigned First Task]
✅ Automated Emails & WhatsApp Alerts guide applicants through each step.

💻 5. Professional & Service Provider Dashboard
Once onboarded, each Professional/service provider gets one unified dashboard.
📌 Key Widgets:
 Task Queue: List of pending tasks (e.g., "Verify title deed for Landlord A's plot")
 Payment Status: How much they've earned, what's pending
 Ratings & Feedback: Client & Professional supervisor feedback
 Performance Metrics: Average completion time, success rate
 Compliance Reminders: e.g., "Police Certificate expiring in 30 days"

🔄 6. Task Assignment Logic
 Internal Professionals:
Assigned automatically for sensitive/high-value transactions.
 External Professionals & Providers:
Assigned based on:
 Location (closest provider first)
 Specialty (electrician for wiring, surveyor for boundary checks)
 Ratings (only 4★+ providers for premium clients)
✅ Admin Override:
Super admin can manually reassign tasks.

⭐ 7. Performance Management
 Task Ratings (1 - 5 Stars):
Every job is rated by:
 Tenant
 Landlord
 Internal supervisor (if assigned)
 Thresholds for Continued Work:
 4★+ average: Eligible for bonuses
 <3★ average: Trigger review, possible suspension
 Automated Warnings:
If deadlines are missed repeatedly, provider is flagged.

💰 8. Payment Flow
 Professional or provider marks task complete --> uploads photos/documents.
 Secure Living Professional approves (for providers) OR admin approves (for Professionals).
 Payment auto-released from landlord wallet.
✅ Example Payment Note for a Plumber:
"Plumber XYZ completed the bathroom repair. Approved by Professional Jane. KSh 5,000 released."

🛡 9. Safeguards & Integrity
 No direct payments to providers  --  all payments flow through Secure Living wallet.
 Dual verification for sensitive work (Professional confirms, admin signs off).
 Random audits: 10% of Professional/provider submissions are spot-checked.

🌟 10. Advanced Features for Future Phases
 Gamified Badges for Providers:
"Gold Plumber," "5-Star Professional" badges increase trust.
 Professional Supervision Layer:
Internal Professionals can audit external Professionals (especially for high-value land verification).
 Live Map Tracking:
Clients can see "Professional Jane is en route to site" (Google Maps integration).
 E-Learning Hub:
Refreshers on fraud detection, customer etiquette, etc.

📊 11. Developer Implementation Notes
 Build ONE dashboard for Professionals & providers, with role-based toggles:
 Plumber sees only repair requests
 Professional sees only verification tasks
 Integrate task assignment algorithm:
 First filter: location
 Second: specialty
 Third: performance rating
 Add payment triggers linked to approvals:
 Repair photo --> Professional approval --> payment
 Verification milestone --> admin approval --> payment
 Ensure multi-language toggle:
 English for diaspora
 Swahili for providers

Here's Section 18: Tenant & Seller Ecosystem Integration  -  expanding how tenants, sellers, and buyers will interact with Secure Living's system, including dashboards, workflows, and how Secure Living keeps control of these relationships for integrity and transparency.

Section 18: Tenant & Seller Ecosystem Integration

🎯 Purpose
Secure Living is not just for landlords and Professionals  --  the ecosystem must also accommodate tenants, sellers, and buyers who interact with the platform daily.
The goal is to ensure:
 ✅ Transparency for all sides (no "hidden deals" or confusion).
 ✅ Control for Secure Living (no bypassing the platform for payments or services).
 ✅ Seamless workflows so tenants, sellers, and buyers feel supported rather than burdened.

🏠 1. Tenant Ecosystem
Tenants are the heartbeat of recurring income for landlords, so Secure Living's system must make their experience smooth, yet controlled.

🔹 Tenant Dashboard Features
Each tenant gets their own login to view everything relevant to their tenancy.
Widgets:
 Rent Status: See if rent is due, partially paid, or cleared.
 Payment Button: Pay via M-Pesa, card, or bank.
 Receipts Archive: Download past rent receipts.
 Maintenance Requests: Submit repair requests (with photo upload).
 Lease Details: Download lease agreement, renewal status.
 Communication Thread: Secure messages with landlord/Professional (monitored by Secure Living). 
 *  Rent payment history & receipts.
 *  Submit vacating notice.
 *  Submit maintenance requests.
 *  Refund status tracker.
 *  Notifications about compliance affecting them (e.g., water outage notice).
   

🔄 Tenant Workflow
flowchart TD
    A[Tenant Logs In] --> B[Checks Rent Status]
    B -->|Needs Payment| C[Pay via Wallet Gateway]
    C --> D[Funds Go to Trust Account]
    D --> E[Landlord Wallet Updates]
    B -->|Submits Repair Request| F[Professional Receives Alert]
    F --> G[Service Provider Dispatched]
    G --> H[Work Complete + Photos Uploaded]
    H --> I[Tenant Confirms / Professional Approves]
✅ Key Feature:
Tenants cannot see escrow or landlord details directly  --  all payments and requests route through Secure Living.

💡 Special Functions for Tenants
 Rent Reminders: SMS & WhatsApp alerts before due date.
 Partial Payments: Tenant can pay in installments (landlord sees live status).
 Damage Reporting: Tenants upload photos of damages  --  triggers service provider workflows.

🏷 2. Seller Ecosystem
Unlike tenants, sellers are not long-term users; they come for a one-time transaction (selling land or property).
Secure Living must prevent this from feeling like a free-for-all marketplace while keeping integrity.

🔹 Seller Dashboard Features
 Document Upload Area: Title deed, ID, KRA PIN, survey map.
 Verification Status Bar: See which documents are "Under Review," "Approved," or "Rejected."
 Communication Tab: Interact with assigned Professional (buyer sees conversation read-only).
 Payment Status: When funds are released (partial/full).
 Checklist Progress:
 ✅ Title Deed Verified
 ✅ GIS Check Complete
 ✅ Sale Agreement Signed

🔄 Seller Workflow
flowchart TD
    A[Seller Registers] --> B[Uploads Title Deed & KRA PIN]
    B --> C[Professional Verifies Docs + GIS Check]
    C --> D[Buyer Observes Progress]
    D --> E[Funds Move from Buyer Wallet to Seller]
✅ Key Feature:
Sellers never receive direct payments  --  all funds move through Secure Living Wallet --> Verification --> Seller Wallet.

🔐 Seller Safeguards
 Locked Communication: Sellers can talk to Professionals, but not buyers directly (prevents side deals).
 Payment Freeze Option: If Professional or buyer spots fraud, funds lock instantly.
 KRA PIN Requirement: Ensures seller tax compliance (and builds Secure Living's reputation).

🛒 3. Buyer Ecosystem
Buyers come for land, houses, or even renovations.
They can:
 Request due diligence (title verification, GIS check).
 Request Professional oversight for construction or purchase.
 Pay through Secure Living wallet  --  funds released only after milestones.

🔹 Buyer Dashboard Features
 Funds in Wallet: Amount deposited for purchase.
 Verification Timeline: Step-by-step progress (title check, survey visit, etc.).
 Professional Reports: PDF reports & video evidence uploaded.
 Dispute Button: Freezes transaction for review by Secure Living admin.
 Add-On Services: e.g., Buyer clicks "Request Staging After Purchase" or "Add Renovation Oversight."

🔄 Buyer Workflow
flowchart TD
    A[Buyer Selects Service] --> B[Deposits Funds in Wallet]
    B --> C[Professional Assigned for Due Diligence]
    C --> D[Verification Milestones Completed]
    D -->|OK| E[Funds Released to Seller]
    D -->|Issue| F[Funds Frozen for Mediation]
✅ Key Feature:
Buyers see everything, but they cannot release funds themselves  --  they tick approvals for milestones, which triggers Secure Living wallet rules.

🌍 4. Airbnb Guest Sub-Ecosystem
Secure Living can manage Airbnb units for landlords. Guests will NOT get their own full dashboard (avoids system complexity).
✅ Instead:
 Professional managing Airbnb shares guest login info for check-in/out.
 Guests pay via Airbnb platform or Secure Living direct gateway.
 Landlords toggle "Airbnb Mode" in property settings to activate special workflows (cleaning, staging, guest reports).

📦 5. Service Provider Coordination
All service providers (plumbers, cleaners, etc.) are connected to tenants, buyers, and sellers via Professionals.
✅ Example Flow:
 Tenant requests repair --> Professional assigns plumber --> Plumber fixes --> Professional approves --> Wallet releases funds.
If tenant is paying for repair:
 Tenant's payment triggers wallet workflow --> Secure Living deducts coordination fee (e.g., 10%) --> Provider is paid balance.

📊 6. Developer Implementation Notes
 Role-Based Dashboards:
 Landlord, Tenant, Seller, Buyer, Professional, Service Provider  --  one system, different views.
 Buyer-Seller Communication Controls:
 Professional mediates; Secure Living monitors all threads.
 Wallet Logic:
 Tenant --> Rent wallet
 Buyer --> Purchase wallet
 Provider --> Task wallet
 Swahili Option for Sellers/Tenants:
 Default English for diaspora buyers.
 Notifications:
 WhatsApp, SMS, email at every milestone.:



Section 19: Hybrid Utility & Service Provider Management
This section expands the flowchart into full operational guidance for developers, investors, and Secure Living admins  --  showing exactly how utilities, trash collection, and small-scale services are managed under Secure Living's hybrid payment model.

📌 Purpose
To create a transparent, flexible, and scalable system for handling utilities and recurring service providers (trash, water, cleaning crews) while maintaining clear records for landlords and tenants.

🔄 Operational Model
Secure Living uses a hybrid utility system:
 Option A: Direct-to-Utility Integration  -  For larger providers (e.g., Nairobi Water, KPLC).
 Option B: Professional-Managed Services  -  For local vendors (trash collectors, borehole water, compound cleaners).
This hybrid setup means Secure Living can adapt to different property types  --  urban apartments with formal utilities, rural properties relying on local providers, and even short-term rentals.

🏗 System Roles & Dashboards
Role
What They See in Dashboard
Landlord
Monthly statements for water, power, trash, etc. Ability to approve/auto-approve payments.
Tenant
Payment history (utilities & rent combined or separated), proof of payment uploads.
Professional
Task view for vendor payments, receipt uploads, dispute reporting.
Service Provider
Mini-portal to upload receipts, view payments owed, confirm service delivery.
Admin
Master oversight: payment logs, vendor performance, reconciliation tools.

📋 Key Features
1️⃣ Direct Utility Payments (Option A)
 Ideal for: Nairobi Water, KPLC, Internet (Safaricom Fiber).
 Process:
 Tenant/landlord logs into Secure Living dashboard.
 Selects "Pay Water Bill" --> enters account number.
 Payment sent directly to utility via integrated API (M-Pesa Paybill, card, bank transfer).
 Receipt generated --> sent to both tenant and landlord dashboards.
 Secure Living earns a small coordination fee (e.g., 1% service fee or fixed Ksh 50).
✅ Developer note:
 API integration required for major utility companies.
 Payment status (pending/complete) auto-syncs to ledger.

2️⃣ Professional-Managed Payments (Option B)
 Ideal for: Rural water vendors, local trash collectors, estate caretakers.
 Process:
 Tenant/landlord pays into Secure Living wallet (tagged for "trash" or "water").
 Professional receives payment task --> pays vendor (cash or M-Pesa).
 Professional uploads proof:
 📸 Photo of receipt or M-Pesa confirmation.
 📄 Optional vendor signature or stamp.
 Dashboard updates automatically (tenant, landlord, and admin see "Vendor Paid").
✅ Benefits:
 Keeps local vendors in the loop even if they lack tech.
 Allows Secure Living to standardize payments and maintain records.

3️⃣ Secure Living Earnings from Utilities
 Coordination Fee: A small margin (1 - 3%) for Professional-managed payments.
 Vendor Subscription (Phase 2): Local vendors can pay Secure Living to be "verified," gaining trust and more clients.
 Premium Reports: Landlords can pay extra for utility analytics (e.g., water usage trends).

📊 Example Scenarios
Scenario 1: Nairobi Apartment
 Water bill (Ksh 2,800) paid directly via Nairobi Water API.
 Tenant gets SMS receipt instantly.
 Secure Living charges Ksh 50 "coordination fee."
Scenario 2: Rural Plot
 Trash collector doesn't have Paybill.
 Tenant pays Ksh 500 via Secure Living dashboard.
 Professional pays trash collector in cash, uploads receipt.
 Landlord sees "Trash Paid  -  Ksh 500" with receipt image.

⚠️ Risks & Mitigations
Risk
Mitigation
Vendor fraud (fake invoices)
Professional uploads proof + Secure Living audits monthly.
Double payments (tenant & landlord both pay)
System auto-checks ledger for duplicates.
Local vendors refusing tech
Professional handles payment manually, keeps them included.

🛠 Developer Guidance
 APIs Needed:
 M-Pesa Daraja API (core backbone).
 Optional utility APIs (Nairobi Water, Safaricom).
 Professional Upload System:
 File upload (PDF/JPG) + metadata (date, vendor, amount).
 Auto-notification to landlord/tenant dashboards.
 Vendor Mini-Action Panels (Future Phase):
 Vendors can log in, confirm payment, upload receipts themselves.

📈 Future Expansions
 Utility Wallet: Tenants can prepay Ksh 5,000 --> automatically deducted monthly.
 Bulk Vendor Deals: Secure Living can negotiate cheaper trash collection for entire estates.
 Water Meter API Integration: Automated reading --> bill calculation --> payment request.
🏢 Unified Executive, Branch Manager & Supervisor Dashboard
(Role-Based Views with Taxonomy Integration + Escalation Workflows)

🎯 Core Principle
There is only one dashboard system. The difference in user experience comes from:
 Role-Based Filters --> Executive, Branch Manager, Supervisor, Staff.
 Taxonomy Scope --> Branch (branch_id), Region (region_id), or Service Type (taxon_service_type).
This approach guarantees:
 ✅ Single source of truth (no mismatched data across Action Panels).
 ✅ Easy promotion path (role change = new view, same system).
 ✅ Development efficiency (one codebase to maintain, test, and secure).

1. Role-Based Views
Executive View
 Scope: All branches + all service types.
 KPIs:
 Global Health Score (Financial 50%, Operational 30%, Compliance 20%).
 Cash Flow Velocity (days receivable vs payable).
 Vacancy Forecasting (cross-region).
 Market Heatmaps (expansion, churn risk).
 Actions:
 Approve budgets above branch thresholds (>KES 500,000).
 Override workflows (finance, ops, compliance).
 Launch enterprise-wide A/B tests (e.g., service bundles).
 Strategic fast-tracks (new partnerships, expansion).

Branch Manager View
 Scope: Assigned branch only (branch_id).
 KPIs:
 Branch Health Score.
 Occupancy Rate vs Company Average.
 Branch-Specific Churn Risk.
 Branch-Level Cash Flow & Receivables.
 Actions:
 Approve spending <=KES 100,000.
 Reassign staff across their branch.
 Escalate branch issues to HQ.
 Run local marketing campaigns.

Supervisor View
 Scope: Assigned service category (taxon_service_type) or property cluster.
 KPIs:
 Task Completion Rate.
 Inspection SLA Timelines.
 Complaint Resolution Metrics.
 Actions:
 Approve/Reject service quotations.
 Override staff or tenant actions (with justification).
 Escalate to Branch Manager if limits exceeded.
 Manage on-ground service providers.

2. Role & Scope Assignment (Admin Panel)
Admin Workflow:
 Admin --> User Management --> Select User.
 Assign Role: Executive, Branch Manager, Supervisor, or Staff.
 Assign Scope:
 Branch (branch_id).
 Region (region_id).
 Service Type (taxon_service_type).
 Optional Approval Limit (KES).
 Save --> System loads correct dashboard view on next login.
Wireframe Example:
┌──────────────────────────────┐
│   USER ROLE ASSIGNMENT       │
├──────────────────────────────┤
│ Select User:  [ Mary N.  v ] │
│ Assign Role:  [ Branch Mgr ] │
│ Scope:        [ Nairobi_West ] 
│ Service Taxa: [ Residential  ] 
│ Approval Limit: [100,000]    │
│                              │
│ [ Save Role ]   [ Cancel ]   │
└──────────────────────────────┘

3. Dashboard Layout (Role-Adaptive)
Shared Layout Skeleton (applies to all roles, with content adapted):
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ SECURE LIVING DASHBOARD                 │
├─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ TOP BAR: Role | Scope | Health Score    │
├────────────────┬────────────────┬───────┤
│ HEALTH OVERVIEW│ FINANCIAL PULSE│ ALERTS│
│ KPIs by role   │ Cash Flow      │ 🔴⚠️   │
├────────────────┴────────────────┴───────┤
│ STRATEGIC INSIGHTS (Vacancy, Trends)    │
├────────────────┬────────────────────────┤
│ PERFORMANCE    │ ACTION PANEL            │
│ Staff, Ops     │ Approve, Escalate, Chat │
└────────────────┴────────────────────────┘
Differences in Views:
 Executive Top Bar --> Health Score 🟢 88%, Global Scope, 5 active alerts.
 Branch Manager Top Bar --> Branch Score 🟡 72% (Nairobi_West), 2 local alerts.
 Supervisor Top Bar --> SLA Compliance 85%, Service Category: Residential.

4. Flow Diagram: Role Detection --> Dashboard

     │
     ▼

     │
     ▼

     ▼

5. Technical Implementation Notes
 Frontend (React/Next.js)
<SecureLivingDashboard 
   role="branch_manager"
   branchId="nairobi_west"
   serviceCategory="residential"
   approvalLimit={100000}
/>
 Database Queries:
-- Executive
SELECT * FROM metrics;

-- Branch Manager
SELECT * FROM metrics WHERE branch_id = 'nairobi_west';

-- Supervisor
SELECT * FROM jobs WHERE category_id = 'residential';
 Health Score Algorithm:
overall_health = (financial * 0.5) 
               + (operational * 0.3) 
               + (compliance * 0.2);
 Audit Trails:
Every action --> stored in immutable audit_logs table with:
user_id, role, scope, action, payload, timestamp, outcome.

6. Accountability & Escalation Rules
 Approval Limits:
 Supervisor --> <= KES 20k
 Branch Manager --> <= KES 100k
 Executive --> Unlimited
 Escalation Workflow:
 Supervisor request >20k --> auto-routed to Branch Manager.
 Branch Manager request >100k --> auto-routed to Executive.
 Team Collaboration:
 In-app chat & comments tied to tasks.
 Notifications at every escalation.
 Promotion Path:
 Mary (Branch Manager) --> promoted to Executive.
 Only role changes; dashboard adapts automatically.

✅ Developer Deliverables
 One codebase (no duplicate dashboards).
 Role-based props & DB filters.
 Configurable taxonomy (branch, region, service).
 Escalation & approval workflow enforced in middleware.
 Immutable audit logging.
 Wireframes + flow diagrams to guide UI/UX.








Section 20 (Extended)  --  Unified Role-Based Dashboard: All Participants (Tenants, Landlords, Staff, Professionals, External Clients)
This is the developer-facing, production-ready expansion describing how every participant type (tenants, landlords, staff, professionals, external clients) maps into the single dashboard platform via roles + scopes + taxonomy. It contains data-model guidance, UI/UX contracts, workflows, security controls, and acceptance tests  --  everything your development team will need to implement role-based views that feel like separate Action Panels but are in fact one unified system.

Purpose & high-level principle
 One platform, many views. Every user logs into the same app. The system computes a user's role and scope and renders a role-appropriate dashboard and feature set.
 Roles are first-class. Roles include: tenant, landlord, staff, supervisor, branch_manager, executive, professional, vendor, external_client. Roles can be combined (a user can be a landlord + professional).
 Scopes are taxonomic. Scope dimensions: branch_id, region_id, property_id, service_category (taxon_service_type), portfolio_id. Permissions are enforced at API, service, and DB layer (row-level).
 Single source of truth. All KPIs, documents, messages, and transactions originate in one data model with immutable audit logging.

Section structure (what follows)
 Role definitions & intended UX for each participant (Tenant, Landlord, Staff, Supervisor, Branch Manager, Executive, Professional, Vendor, External Client).
 Shared components & behavior (login --> role detection --> view rendering, audit, notifications, feature flags).
 Data model additions & schema examples for participant types.
 Key workflows (onboarding, provider onboarding & verification, tenant vacating --> auto-listing, job --> quote --> escrow --> milestone payments).
 RBAC, API contracts & enforcement strategies (including RLS concepts).
 Security, privacy & compliance notes for PII and financial flows.
 Performance, search & geospatial hints.
 Acceptance tests & developer checklist.

1  --  Role definitions & UX / feature breakdown
Below each role includes: purpose, scope, dashboard modules, KPIs, actions, typical UI components, and sample notifications.
Tenant (role: tenant)
Purpose: pay rent, raise maintenance, receive notices, submit vacating notices, view receipts.
Scope: lease_id, property_id, tenant's personal profile only.
Dashboard modules:
 Rent status & upcoming payment widget (amount, due date, pay button).
 Maintenance requests (status, chat with supervisor/professional).
 Noticeboard (landlord & manager notices).
 Vacating notice form + deposit refund tracker.
 Lease documents & signed agreements.
 Saved searches / Notify Me (for prospects switching houses).
KPIs:
 Payment timeliness.
 Number of open maintenance tickets.
 NPS / satisfaction prompts.
Actions:
 Pay via wallet / M-Pesa / bank.
 Submit maintenance with photos & geotag.
 Submit vacating notice (choose date; system enforces configured notice period).
 Download receipts & tax documents (if allowed).
Typical notifications:
 Rent due reminders (D-7, D-3, D-1).
 Maintenance updates (assigned, started, completed).
 Vacating acceptance and inspection scheduling.
UI components & behavior rules:
 Minimal, mobile-first UI.
 Require explicit consent for PII use and image uploads.
 Tenant cannot see other tenants' data or landlord financials.

Landlord (role: landlord)
Purpose: manage property portfolio, view income, approve major maintenance, receive statements, request premium services.
Scope: portfolio_id, list of property_id that belong to landlord.
Dashboard modules:
 Portfolio overview (units, occupancy, rent collected).
 Escrow & payout schedule (detailed ledger).
 Maintenance approvals & quotations.
 Tenant applications & tenant screening results.
 Document vault (title deeds, compliance docs).
 Marketplace access (toggle professional marketplace module).
 Notifications & alerts (tax deadlines, compliance).
KPIs:
 Occupancy %, average rent per unit, arrears %, time-to-repair average, net payouts.
Actions:
 Approve/decline maintenance quotes above auto-approve threshold.
 Toggle premium modules (tax support, inspection, security).
 Approve disbursements from escrow (within rules).
 Invite participants to property group (co-owners, advisors) and assign roles inside project workspaces.
Typical notifications:
 Rent collected (per unit).
 Maintenance quote ready for approval.
 Reserve fund below minimum.
 Listing performance & prospect counts (if listing active).
UI components & behavior rules:
 Landlord's dashboard shows aggregated per-portfolio and per-unit views.
 Landlord can enable Advance Payout Guarantee (if eligible).
 Landlord can invite participants; invited users are assigned project-specific roles.

Staff (role: staff)
Purpose: execute tasks assigned by supervisors or managers  --  inspections, maintenance, tenant outreach.
Scope: assigned job_ids, property clusters, or branch.
Dashboard modules:
 My tasks (today, upcoming, overdue).
 Routing & maps (if field work).
 Evidence upload (geotagged photos, signature capture).
 Quick chat with supervisor / landlord.
KPIs:
 Tasks completed, SLA compliance, average resolution time.
Actions:
 Mark tasks as in progress / blocked / complete.
 Upload evidence & submit for supervisor verification.
 Request escalation for approvals.
UI components & behavior rules:
 Mobile-first, offline-first capture of photos & data with sync queue.
 Limited permissions (no financial approvals).
 Access to scheduling/calendar only for jobs they own.

Supervisor (role: supervisor)
Purpose: quality control, verify jobs, schedule inspections, approve small spend, escalate.
Scope: taxon_service_type (e.g., inspections, maintenance) and assigned properties/clusters.
Dashboard modules:
 Assignments & workload view.
 Inspection scheduling & checklist.
 Quotation approval queue (within threshold).
 Professional performance & vendor scorecards.
KPIs:
 First time approval rate, average inspection turnaround, rework rate.
Actions:
 Approve small quotes (configured threshold, e.g., <= KES 20k).
 Schedule & confirm inspections.
 Escalate major approvals to Branch Manager.
 Flag vendors for retraining or suspension.
UI components & behavior rules:
 Detailed inspection checklists and mandatory evidence types for each milestone.

Branch Manager (role: branch_manager)
Purpose: branch P&L, manage supervisors & staff, approve operations spend within limits, escalate to HQ.
Scope: a single branch_id or a set of branches.
Dashboard modules:
 Branch financials, occupancy & rent collection.
 Vendor aging & OPEX runway per branch.
 Staff roster & capacity.
 Local alert center (compliance, disputes).
KPIs:
 Branch Health Score, arrears percentage, Opex runway, vendor performance.
Actions:
 Approve branch-level budgets <= configured limit (e.g., KES 100k).
 Reassign staff & resources.
 Approve marketing spend for local campaigns.

Executive (role: executive)
Purpose: company-level control & strategy.
Scope: Global (no branch filter), can drill into branches.
Dashboard modules:
 Global Health Score & breakdown.
 Strategic insights: market heatmaps, vacancy forecasting across regions.
 Alerts & compliance threats.
 Approvals for company-wide budgets & partnership approvals.
Actions:
 Approve budgets > branch limits.
 Override workflows and immediate responses for critical events.

Professional (role: professional)  --  internal or external
Purpose: deliver expert services  --  surveyors, lawyers, engineers, contractors.
Scope: assigned job_ids, service categories, coverage areas.
Dashboard modules:
 Job queue, quotes, assigned milestones.
 Document uploads & proof collector.
 Performance & payment history.
KPIs:
 Completion rate, average quoting time, client rating.
Actions:
 Accept/decline assignments.
 Submit quotes (versions), revisions.
 Submit milestone evidence to trigger payment.
Verification & trust:
 Multi-tier verification: basic (ID), premium (license, insurance), elite (references, site checks).
 Verification records stored and surfaced to landlords & staff.

Vendor (role: vendor)
Purpose: supply goods & invoices.
Scope: branch-specific contracts or global.
Dashboard modules:
 Invoices, payments, job history, listing (for premium providers).
 Proposal & quote submission.
Actions:
 Upload invoices, manage tax documents, respond to RFQs.

External Client (role: external_client)
Purpose: users who are not landlords but need one-time services (due diligence, land verification, proxy representation).
Scope: project-level only.
Dashboard modules:
 Project tracker, escrow status, delivered reports.
 Proposal & quotation management.
Actions:
 Request services, approve quotes, fund escrow, accept reports.

2  --  Shared platform behaviors & flows
Login --> Role detection --> View rendering (single flow)
 User authenticates (SSO/OAuth or local).
 Backend resolves user_id --> fetch user_roles and role_scopes (from cache).
 Server calculates effective_scope (list of branch_ids/property_ids/service categories).
 Middleware attaches req.effective_scope or issues scope_token.
 Frontend receives role & scope in auth payload and renders UI with role-specific feature flags.
 All API calls include scope implicitly (server enforces)  --  front-end never trusted for scope enforcement.
ASCII flow:
[Login] -> Auth Service -> Get Roles & Scopes -> Role Middleware -> Apply Filters -> Render Dashboard
Notifications & alert behavior
 Notifications table persists all notifications with recipient_role, recipient_ids, branch_id, severity, payload.
 Channels: in-app push, email (diaspora), SMS/WhatsApp (opt-in).
 Alerts should include suggested actions & deep links to relevant screen.
Audit trails
 Every immutable action writes to audit_logs with user_id, role, scope, endpoint, action, payload_hash, created_at.
 Audit logs are append-only; retention policy 7+ years for financial actions.

3  --  Data model & schema guidance (developer-ready)
Below are recommended tables/fields to support role-based behavior. Use JSONB for flexible attributes. Adjust types to your stack.
Core user/role tables
users (
  id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  email TEXT UNIQUE,
  name TEXT,
  password_hash TEXT NULL,
  primary_branch_id INT NULL,
  is_active BOOLEAN DEFAULT true,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
  profile JSONB
);

roles (id serial PK, name text unique)  -- e.g., tenant, landlord, staff, supervisor, branch_manager, executive, professional, vendor, external_client

user_roles (
  id serial PK,
  user_id UUID REFERENCES users(id),
  role_id INT REFERENCES roles(id),
  assigned_by UUID,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);

role_scopes (
  id serial PK,
  user_role_id INT REFERENCES user_roles(id),
  branch_id INT NULL,
  property_id INT NULL,
  portfolio_id INT NULL,
  region_id INT NULL,
  service_taxon TEXT NULL,
  approval_limit NUMERIC NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);
Tenants & landlords relation
tenancies (
  id UUID PK,
  lease_id UUID UNIQUE,
  property_id INT REFERENCES properties(id),
  unit_id INT,
  tenant_id UUID REFERENCES users(id),
  rent_amount NUMERIC,
  start_date DATE,
  end_date DATE NULL,
  status ENUM('active','terminated','pending'),
  deposit_amount NUMERIC,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);

landlord_portfolios (
  id UUID PK,
  landlord_id UUID REFERENCES users(id),
  portfolio_name TEXT,
  properties JSONB,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);
Professionals / providers
service_providers (
  id UUID PK,
  user_id UUID REFERENCES users(id) NULL,
  company_name TEXT,
  categories TEXT[] , -- ['plumbing','survey']
  coverage_areas JSONB, -- ['Nairobi','Kiambu']
  verification_status ENUM('basic','premium','elite'),
  performance JSONB (avg_rating, jobs_completed),
  pricing_tiers JSONB,
  verification_docs JSONB,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);
Marketplace & jobs
provider_jobs (
  id UUID PK,
  requester_id UUID, -- landlord or external client
  property_id INT,
  category TEXT,
  urgency ENUM('low','medium','high'),
  budget_min NUMERIC,
  budget_max NUMERIC,
  status ENUM('posted','quoted','assigned','in_progress','completed','disputed','paid'),
  escrow_hold_id UUID NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);

job_quotes (
  id UUID PK,
  job_id UUID REFERENCES provider_jobs(id),
  provider_id UUID REFERENCES service_providers(id),
  amount NUMERIC,
  message TEXT,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);
Escrow & wallet
wallets (
  id UUID PK,
  owner_type ENUM('landlord','platform','provider','staff'),
  owner_id UUID,
  balance NUMERIC,
  currency TEXT,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);

escrow_holds (
  id UUID PK,
  wallet_id UUID REFERENCES wallets(id),
  job_id UUID REFERENCES provider_jobs(id) NULL,
  amount NUMERIC,
  status ENUM('held','released','refunded','disputed'),
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);
Listings & auto-listing
property_listings (
  id UUID PK,
  property_id INT,
  listing_type ENUM('public','private'),
  status ENUM('draft','scheduled','active','rented','paused'),
  available_from DATE,
  premium_features JSONB,
  media JSONB, -- images/videos metadata
  search_metadata JSONB,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);

saved_searches (
  id UUID PK,
  user_id UUID,
  criteria JSONB, -- rent_range, radius, amenities
  notify_channel TEXT[] -- ['inapp','email','sms']
);
Notifications & audit
notifications (id, recipient_type, recipient_id, branch_id, type, severity, payload JSONB, acknowledged_by, acknowledged_at, created_at)

audit_logs (id BIGSERIAL PK, user_id UUID, role TEXT, action TEXT, scope JSONB, payload_hash TEXT, ip INET, created_at TIMESTAMPTZ)
Indexing notes: index branch_id, property_id, and created_at on large tables. Use GiST for geometry/geography columns.

4  --  Key workflows (detailed)
A  --  Tenant vacating --> auto-listing flow (detailed)
Trigger: Tenant submits vacating notice (tenant app).
Steps:
 Tenant fills vacating form: planned_move_out_date, reason_for_leaving, forwarding_address, refund_channel.
 System validates notice length (e.g., 30-day default). If less than minimum, show error (unless supervisor override on abandonment).
 System flags lease as ending_on and sets unit_status = upcoming_vacancy.
 Supervisor receives task: Pre-vacation inspection scheduled automatically (calendar invite).
 On inspection completion:
 Inspector uploads photos & rates condition_score.
 If condition_score >= threshold and latest photos < 12 months old, create property_listing draft with available_from = planned_move_out_date.
 If photos older or condition_score low --> prompt supervisor to upload fresh media.
 Landlord receives draft listing notification; landlord can select premium listing options in advance (marketing spend).
 If tenant abandons unit (no notice):
 Supervisor tags vacant_after_inspection, listing becomes available_now.
 Deposit refund remains on_hold until inspection confirms condition.
 System sends saved-search notifications to prospects matching criteria (via saved_searches).
 If listing converts to rented, update tenancies and close listing.
Important rules:
 Only supervisor or above can mark a unit available_now without tenant notice.
 Listing creation includes source_event_id (vacating_notice) for audit.
Notifications & data captured for intelligence:
 vacating reason (categorical), decline reasons after tours, time-to-list, price change history.

B  --  Provider job & escrow lifecycle
 Landlord / external client posts provider_job (category, budget, location).
 Providers respond with job_quotes.
 Landlord selects a quote or provider; funds are moved to escrow_hold.
 Provider executes work, submits milestone evidence (geotagged photos).
 Supervisor/landlord verifies milestones; upon approval, escrow releases payment per milestone to provider wallet and platform takes commission (record in provider_commissions).
 Dispute handling path: open provider_dispute record; admin can mediate. Dispute holds funds until resolution.
Quality & verification:
 Providers must meet verification tier for job types (insurance threshold).
 High-value jobs require multi-party approvals (landlord + co-owner + supervisor).

C  --  Saved search / prospect notification matching
 saved_searches persisted; when property_listing.status changes to draft/scheduled/active, the matching function queries saved searches in the radius and criteria and enqueues notifications for matching prospects.
 Notifications include link to listing; prospects can subscribe to alerts or book a viewing.

5  --  RBAC & enforcement
Principle:
 Server-side authoritative enforcement. Never rely on front-end to pass scope filters.
 Use a combination of:
 Application middleware (resolve user_roles and role_scopes, set req.effective_scope).
 ORM Query Wrapper / Global Scopes that apply WHERE branch_id IN (effective_scope.branches) to every transactional table.
 DB row-level security (RLS) policies (Postgres) for defense-in-depth: each API call runs as a service user, and application passes current_user_id via session variable; RLS policy uses that variable to allow only rows where branch_id in allowed set returned from a permission function.
Example RLS concept (Postgres)
 Create function get_allowed_branches(user_id) that returns integer[] (cached result).
 Policy for maintenance_requests: using (branch_id = ANY(get_allowed_branches(current_setting('app.current_user')::uuid))).
Permission model
 permissions table maps role_id to branch_id or NULL (NULL = global).
 user_roles join maps user_id -> role_id.
 Effective allowed branches computed by joining user_roles -> permissions.

6  --  API contract examples (representative, not exhaustive)
Auth response (on login)
{
  "user_id": "uuid",
  "roles": ["landlord","branch_manager"],
  "scopes": { "branches":[1], "properties":[101,102], "service_taxa":["residential"] },
  "approval_limits": { "maintenance": 100000, "vendor_payments": 100000 }
}
Get listings (server enforces scope)
GET /api/listings?format=json
Headers: Authorization: Bearer <token>
Response.meta.effective_scope = [branch_id,...]
Post maintenance request
POST /api/maintenance_requests
Body: { property_id, unit_id, category, description, photos[] }
Server validates: user has access to property_id, timestamps, geotag.
Job quote
POST /api/provider_jobs/{job_id}/quotes
- provider must be verified for category.

7  --  Security & privacy
 Encryption in transit & at rest. TLS, HSTS, with DB-level encryption for sensitive fields (KYC docs, national IDs).
 PII minimization: store hashed or encrypted IDs with access controls; store only necessary fields for functionality.
 MFA for high-privilege roles. Enforce 2FA for executive, finance, and admin users.
 Logging & monitoring. ELK/Datadog for suspicious patterns, repeated 403 attempts.
 Backups & retention. Offsite encrypted backups, RTO/RPO documented. Audit logs retention >= 7 years for financial trails.
 Consent & transparency. Users must opt-in to data collection where required (photos, device location). Show privacy policy and allowed uses (analytics, product improvement).

8  --  Performance, search & geospatial notes
 Search & discovery:
 Use an external search index (Elasticsearch / OpenSearch) for full-text and faceted search.
 Maintain listing_search_index with location_point fields for spatial queries.
 Keep search_metadata (amenities normalized) for fast filtering, also maintain a decline_reasons histogram for intelligence.
 Geospatial:
 Store centroid as geography(POINT,4326), boundaries as GeoJSON. Use PostGIS and GiST indexes.
 For map overlays (schools, hospitals), use cached OpenStreetMap tiles and precomputed overlays to avoid API calls.
 Caching & scaling:
 Cache permissions per user (short TTL).
 Use Redis for session & frequent queries (top KPIs).
 Materialized views for heavy aggregates (branch KPIs) refreshed on schedule or on relevant events.

9  --  Testing & acceptance criteria (developer checklist)
Unit & Integration tests
 Middleware enforces req.effective_scope correctly for:
 Executive (global access)  --  can query across branches.
 Branch Manager  --  can query only their branch.
 Supervisor  --  only service taxonomy or assigned properties.
 Tenant  --  only their lease/property.
 RLS policies block unauthorized access when invoked directly.
End-to-end tests
 Tenant vacating flow results in draft listing in DB and saved-search notifications queued.
 Provider job lifecycle (post --> quote --> assign --> escrow hold --> milestone --> release) must pass for happy path and dispute path.
Security tests
 Attempt to change branch_id in API parameters must be rejected.
 High-privilege MFA enforced scenario covered.
Performance tests
 Dashboard global KPI endpoint must respond under target (e.g., < 500ms) with caching and materialized view in place.

10  --  Operational & admin tooling
 Admin UI: Create/edit roles, manage taxonomy, approve provider verifications, generate audit packs.
 Taxonomy manager: Admins can add new service_taxon, property_type, and map them to pricing rules and verification requirements.
 Feature flags: Use feature toggle system (LaunchDarkly / internal flags) to roll out modules (marketplace, GIS features).
 Alerts & runbooks: Map critical alerts (escrow mismatch, runway < X months) to runbooks and responsible owners.

11  --  Example real-life scenarios (illustrative)
Scenario A  --  Diaspora landlord wants renovation + tax support
 Landlord chooses Full-Service + Renovation Module (one-time) + Taxation (recurring).
 Landlord's role scope is portfolio_id; backend provisions landlord_modules record.
 Renovation job posted in Marketplace; vetted professional assigned; escrow hold created; milestones verified by engineer (professional).
Scenario B  --  Tenant gives notice; auto-listing & prospect notifications
 Tenant posts 60-day vacate.
 System schedules inspection; upon verification, draft listing created for available_from.
 100 prospects with saved-search criteria matching area/rent receive notifications.
Scenario C  --  Supervisor escalates budget
 Supervisor posts vendor quote of KES 40k; threshold 20k. System auto-creates approval task for Branch Manager. Branch Manager approves (<=100k)  --  job assigned.

12  --  Suggested docs to include with section (deliverables for dev handoff)
 ER diagram (users, roles, role_scopes, permissions, wallets, provider_jobs).
 OpenAPI endpoints for core flows (auth, listings, maintenance, jobs, escrow).
 Sample SQL migrations for new tables above.
 Wireframes for major role dashboards & role-assignment admin UI.
 Acceptance test scripts (postman collection / curl examples).
 Runbooks for critical alerts (escrow mismatch, security incidents).

Final notes (practical guidance)
 Progressive roll-out: implement core role-based RBAC and branch enforcement first. Start with Tenant, Landlord, Staff and basic Branch Manager features. Add Marketplace/Professional, Escrow, and GIS modules progressively with feature flags.
 Keep server authoritative: always apply scope filtering on server/DB side even if front-end hides controls.
 Make verification tiered: for professionals and providers to enable trust-based matching.
 Instrument for analytics: capture decline reasons, repair brand & item metadata, and provider performance for data intelligence products.





   
   
   

Section 21: Reporting, Analytics & Oversight Framework

📌 Purpose
This section explains how Secure Living will track, analyze, and present all data from the platform -- ensuring landlords, tenants, Professionals, service providers, and admins have access to accurate, real-time insights while maintaining compliance and trust.
This is not just bookkeeping -- it's a full intelligence layer that makes Secure Living scalable, investor-friendly, and highly transparent.

🔍 What the Reporting & Analytics System Covers
 Landlord Financials: Rent collection, deductions, repairs, ROI metrics.
 Tenant Activity: Payment history, arrears, disputes.
 Professional Performance: Task completion rates, average resolution time, client ratings.
 Service Provider Data: Jobs completed, payments processed, quality ratings.
 System Health Metrics: Wallet balance, trust account reconciliation, dispute resolution timelines.

📊 Reporting Modules by Role
Role
What They See in Dashboard
Landlord

Tenant
Payment receipts, outstanding balances, utility breakdowns.
Professional
Task history, performance stats, pending tasks, earnings summary.
Service Provider
Work logs, payments received, rating dashboard.

Full business-wide intelligence (all data aggregated + export tools).

📈 Key Features
1️⃣ Landlord Financial Reporting
 What It Shows:
 Rent collected vs. rent expected.
 Repairs deducted, fees deducted.
 Property portfolio performance (e.g., "3 of your 5 units are occupied  -  95% ROI").
 Developer Guidance:
 Monthly auto-generated PDFs (downloadable + emailed).
 CSV/Excel export for accountants.
 Value Add:
✅ Positions Secure Living as more transparent than competitors like Diaspora Access & Simpl Properties, who mostly provide basic rent statements only.

2️⃣ Professional Performance Analytics
 Each Professional (internal or external) gets a performance profile:

 Use Case:
 Secure Living can reward top Professionals (bonus system).
 Poor-performing Professionals get flagged for retraining or removal.

 Escrow/wallet ledger for disputes.

 Number of jobs done.
 Payment history.
 Client/Professional feedback ratings.

Admins have master access:
