22. Performance & Integrity Monitoring System
To further differentiate Secure Living as the most transparent and trusted diaspora property management brand, the platform will include a Performance & Integrity Monitoring System in Phase 3.

🎯 Objectives
 Guarantee that every transaction and interaction aligns with Secure Living's mission of escrow-first, transparent property management.
 Provide landlords with visibility, accountability, and actionable insights instead of vague reports.
 Detect and fix root causes of cancellations, dissatisfaction, or dishonesty quickly.

🔑 Core KPIs Tracked
🧑‍💼 Staff KPIs
 Escrow Usage Rate  -  % of transactions handled via escrow (automated from logs).
 On-Time Reports Sent  -  % of reports delivered on schedule (automated timestamp check).
 Client Satisfaction (NPS)  -  average score per portfolio, with mandatory qualitative feedback: "Please tell us the main reason for your score."
 Client Retention / Cancellations  -  % of landlords who renew or stay. Every cancellation requires staff to select a structured reason from a dropdown.
⚠️ If one staff member has low retention or repeated cancellation reasons linked to them, it indicates potential dishonesty, incompetence, or escrow bypass collusion.

👷 Professional KPIs
 Task Completion Rate  -  % of jobs completed on time.
 Milestone Accuracy  -  whether approved construction/repair stages pass later inspections.
 Cost vs. Estimate Variance  -  % difference between quoted and actual costs.
 Evidence Upload (Binary)  -  Was the required package (3 photos, 1 video, receipt) uploaded within 24h? Yes/No.
 Escrow Compliance  -  % of their transactions done via escrow.
 Client Ratings  -  1 - 5 stars per task.
 Linked Cancellations  -  cancellation data tied to Professional ID (if multiple landlords quit under one Professional, it triggers investigation).

🏠 Tenant KPIs --> Tenant Reliability Index (TRI)
 Timely Rent Payments  -  % of rent paid on or before due date.
 Escrow Complaints  -  # of disputes about payments or repairs.
 Behavior/Compliance  -  noise, lease violations, property damage.
 Property Condition Score  -  Professional inspection rating (1 - 5) vs. move-in condition.
 TRI Alerts  -  automatic alerts if TRI falls below threshold (e.g., <70%).
⚠️ Turns tenant oversight into a sellable feature for landlords, helping them make renewal decisions based on hard data.

🔹 Why Behind the What  -  Enhanced KPI Logic
 Client Retention KPI
 Retention Rate: >= 90%
 Structured Cancellation Dropdown:
 Cost Too High
 Dissatisfied with Professional (auto-link name)
 Dissatisfied with Staff (auto-link name)
 Service Too Slow / Unresponsive
 Sold Property
 Moved to Competitor (competitor logged)
 Other (must specify)
 Provides patterns, not vague notes (e.g., multiple landlords cancelling due to "Professional James").
 NPS KPI
 Target: >= +40
 Mandatory Follow-Up: "Please tell us the main reason for your score."
 Gives context (e.g., "Reports always late" vs. "Great staff but slow repairs").

🔹 System Design & Safeguards
 Central Dashboard with RAG Status  -  Green, Amber, Red indicators for quick oversight.
 Monthly Reviews  -  KPIs determine bonuses, promotions, or terminations.
 Rewards: Top staff get best portfolios, top Professionals get priority jobs, high-TRI tenants flagged for landlord retention.
 Data Integrity:
 Automated metrics from logs (no self-reporting).
 Tamper-proof audit trails (e.g., "Professional X approved Milestone Y at 14:35 GMT").
 Automated feedback loops after every key client interaction.

🌍 Benefits
 For Clients: Transparent, actionable insights --> no guessing why cancellations or issues happen.
 For Staff/Professionals: Clear accountability + fair rewards system.
 For Secure Living: Strongest differentiator in the Kenyan market  --  a data-driven integrity guarantee.


Financial Management & Monitoring Module
To safeguard Secure Living's growth and ensure long-term sustainability, the system will include a Financial Management & Monitoring Module tightly integrated with the escrow wallet and operational dashboard. This module goes beyond accounting  --  it functions as a financial radar and control system.

🎯 Objectives
 Establish disciplined financial procedures from Day One.
 Provide automated billing, strict credit enforcement, and real-time financial visibility.
 Deliver monthly financial statements and proactive early-warning alerts.
 Ensure resilience through oversight, banking partnerships, and disaster recovery planning.

🔑 Core Financial Procedures
Billing Frequency
 Rent Management Fees: automatically deducted from escrow wallet before landlord payout.
 Concierge/Project Fees: billed upfront deposit + milestone-based billing via dashboard.
Credit Policies
 Strict no-credit policy: all services must be pre-funded in escrow before work begins.
 Only "credit" exposure is the few days between tenant payment and fee deduction.
Tracking "Inventory" (Professional Hours)
 Inventory = billable Professional hours and capacity.
 Dashboard tracks Utilization Rate (% of hours billed vs. available).
 Prevents overwork/underutilization.

📊 Daily & Weekly Financial Pulse Checks
Daily Snapshot (automated):
 Cash Balance (corporate account).
 Escrow Holdings (trust balances).
 Incoming transfers (ACH / Wire / M-Pesa).
 Expected payouts in next 5 days.
Weekly Review Dashboard:
 Revenue Collected (fees auto-deducted).
 Operational Costs (salaries, vendors, SaaS).
 Weekly Profit / Burn Rate.

📑 Mandatory Monthly Deep Dive
System auto-generates three statements for founders/finance manager:
 P&L Statement
 Revenue by service line.
 Professional COGS vs. margin.
 Operating expenses vs. budget.
 Balance Sheet
 Escrow Liabilities separated from Equity.
 Cash vs. Receivables (minimal expected).
 Debt ratio monitored.
 Cash Flow Statement
 Operating Cash Flow (must remain positive).
 Investing & Financing activities tracked separately.
Book Insights on Financial Management (Trust & Escrow)
 Trust Accounts are Mandatory
 Property managers must separate client funds (rent, deposits, reserves) from the company's operating funds.
 Funds must never be mixed, even temporarily.
 Security Deposits Handling
 Deposits are held in trust/escrow until the end of tenancy.
 Managers are required to keep detailed ledgers per tenant/landlord showing deposits, interest (if applicable), and deductions.
 Escrow in Sales/Transfers
 When properties are sold, tenant deposits must be transferred through escrow to ensure the buyer inherits accurate balances.
 Reserve/Repair Accounts
 Landlords often authorize property managers to hold reserve funds (e.g., $500 - $1,000) in trust/escrow to cover repairs and emergencies.
 This prevents delays in fixing urgent issues.
 Audit & Reconciliation
 Monthly reconciliations of trust/escrow accounts are required.
 A 3-way reconciliation: bank statement, client ledger, and master account ledger must all match.
 Records must be auditable and tamper-proof.
 Dispute Handling
 If disputes arise (e.g., deductions from a deposit), funds must remain in escrow until resolution. Deductions can made on each client deposit to repair the house after vacating the premises.
A reserve is money that a landlord authorizes the property manager to hold aside in escrow or trust to cover routine or emergency expenses without needing to call the landlord every time.

📌 How It Works in Practice
 Example: A landlord says, "Keep Ksh 50,000 in my escrow account as a reserve."
 If a pipe bursts or a small repair is needed:
 The proxy requests payment from the reserve.
 Secure Living approves it (if within pre-set rules).
 The contractor is paid immediately from the reserve, without waiting days for the landlord to wire funds from abroad.

📌 Why It Matters
 Speed  -  Urgent repairs (e.g., leaks, security issues) get fixed immediately.
 Trust  -  Landlords don't need to worry about money being mixed up, since it's still in escrow, not in Secure Living's operational account.
 Efficiency  -  Saves proxies from delays and tenants from living with unresolved problems.
 Transparency  -  Every use of the reserve is logged in the landlord's dashboard, with receipts and photos.

📌 Typical Policies (from global best practice)
 Amount: Often 1 month's rent or a set minimum (e.g., $500 in US markets; in Kenya maybe Ksh 30,000 - 100,000).
 Authorization: Manager can only spend from reserves for pre-approved categories (repairs, emergencies, security).
 Reporting: Every deduction must be backed by evidence (photos, invoice, proxy report).

✅ In Secure Living's case:
Reserves would simply be escrow funds tagged "Landlord Reserve" with clear rules on usage. They increase efficiency and reduce the need for constant back-and-forth with diaspora landlords.



Performance & Integrity Monitoring System and now the Sales Compensation Framework. Secure Living's system has two layers of financial flows:
 External Client Flows (Escrow --> landlord payouts, vendor/Professional payments, tenant rent, etc.)
 Internal Compensation Flows (how your own staff, Professionals, and salespeople are paid inside the business).
The "another layer of workers payment" you're asking about would be the internal compensation module, which should sit inside the same platform so payments are:
 Automated (salary + commission + bonuses flow directly from escrow/profit data). Professionals are paid through the escrow wallet following the payment workflows defined in the Professionals Action Panel (Section 4.5.10).
 Linked to KPIs (a salesperson only gets commission when escrow confirms client payment; a Professional only gets bonus if milestones + evidence check out).
 Transparent (staff can see their earnings in their dashboard, but not edit).
 Auditable (finance/HR has a clean log of all payouts).

🔑 The 3 Internal Payment Layers for Secure Living
 Fixed Base Layer (Salary / Retainer)
 For core staff (management, finance, ops).
 For sales team (safety net retainer, milestone increases).
 Paid monthly on schedule.
 Variable Performance Layer (Commission / Bonuses)
 Sales commissions, retention bonuses.
 Professional task-based payments (jobs completed, evidence uploaded, milestones approved).
 Team bonuses (e.g., sales target challenge).
 Strategic Incentives Layer (Long-Term Retention)
 Loyalty bonus (12-month client retention payout).
 Override commissions for managers (when promoted).
 Future stock options/profit-sharing for top leadership.


Internal Payroll & Incentives
1. Salary (Fixed Base Pay)
 Each Internal Professional has a fixed monthly salary, stored in the profiles table.
 Salary is separate from incentives.
 Statutory deductions (PAYE, NHIF, NSSF, levies) auto-applied.
 Payslips must show Gross, Deductions, Net.

2. Incentives (Performance-Based)
Incentives = Standard + (Bonus Plus OR Whale).
2.1 mgmt_fee Calculation
mgmt_fee = agreed_management_percentage * total_rent_collected_for_first_month
 agreed_management_percentage is stored in the contracts table.
 total_rent_collected_for_first_month comes from the trust account ledger.

2.2 Formula
 Standard Reward
standard = standard_rate * mgmt_fee
 Bonus Plus (target met + avg rating >=4.0/5)
bonus_plus = bonus_plus_rate * mgmt_fee
 Whale Bonus (flagged deal, exceptional threshold)
whale = whale_rate * mgmt_fee
 Exclusivity Rule: If Whale = true --> Bonus Plus = 0.
 total_incentive = standard + (bonus_plus OR whale)

2.3 Cap Rule
 max_incentive = cap_rate * mgmt_fee (default 20%)
 final_incentive = min(total_incentive, max_incentive)

2.4 Clawback Policy
 100% clawback if churn <=30 days.
 50% clawback if churn 31 - 90 days.
 0% clawback if >90 days.
 Clawbacks auto-deducted from future payouts.

3. Role-Specific Rates with Versioning
 Rates (standard_rate, bonus_plus_rate, whale_rate, cap_rate) are stored in the roles table.
 Each row has:
 role_id
 standard_rate
 bonus_plus_rate
 whale_rate
 cap_rate
 effective_start_date
 effective_end_date (NULL = active)
🔹 This ensures historical accuracy: old deals keep their original rates, new deals use updated rates.

4. Workflow Logic
 Deal Logged --> contract_id linked to worker_id.
 Revenue Confirmed --> mgmt_fee calculated.
 Fetch Rates --> system selects correct rates using role_id + rate effective date.
 Compute Incentive --> apply Standard + (Bonus Plus OR Whale), then cap.
 Clawback Flag --> mark provisional for 90 days.
 Approval Step
 Normal incentives = auto-approval.
 If Whale flag applied OR Cap triggered --> status = requires_approval.
 Finance Officer/CEO must review & approve before payout.
 Payroll Execution --> Salary + Incentives  -  Clawbacks  -  Deductions = Net Pay.
 Audit Log Entry --> every action recorded.

5. Audit Log (Enhanced with Reasons)
 Stored as JSON blob in audit_log.
 Every change must log:
 timestamp
 user_id
 action (flag, adjustment, approval)
 before/after values
 reason (policy justification)
Example JSON:
[
  {
    "timestamp": "2025-05-15T14:05:00Z",
    "user_id": 12,
    "action": "whale_flag_applied",
    "before": {"bonus_plus": 800, "whale": 0},
    "after": {"bonus_plus": 0, "whale": 12800},
    "reason": "Whale threshold exceeded per policy 4.14"
  },
  {
    "timestamp": "2025-05-16T10:30:00Z",
    "user_id": 3,
    "action": "cap_adjustment",
    "before": {"final_incentive": 20000},
    "after": {"final_incentive": 16000},
    "reason": "Applied 20% cap rule per policy 4.16"
  }
]
This ensures a transparent, immutable record for disputes and audits.

6. Status States
The status field should support:
 pending --> awaiting system calculation
 requires_approval --> Whale or Cap triggered
 approved --> Finance/CEO approved
 paid --> funds disbursed

7. Database (Incentives Table)
 incentive_id
 contract_id
 worker_id
 mgmt_fee
 standard_amount
 bonus_plus_amount
 whale_amount
 total_before_cap
 cap_amount
 final_incentive
 clawback_status (none / 100% / 50%)
 clawback_amount
 status (pending / requires_approval / approved / paid)
 approved_by
 approved_at
 audit_log (JSON blob with reasons)

✅ With these additions, your Internal Payroll system is now:
 Explicit (mgmt_fee, cap, exclusivity)
 Immutable & auditable (JSON + reasons)
 Historically accurate (rate versioning)
 Governance-proof (approval state enforced)
 Developer-ready (formulas, fields, workflow

External Payroll & Incentives 
This module governs how External Professionals (vendors, proxies, contractors, technicians) are compensated.

1. Base Pay (Service Fees & Retainers)
 Most external professionals are commission-only based on services delivered.
 Some external professionals may receive a fixed retainer fee, stored in the profiles table.
 Clarification  -  Retainer Model:
 The retainer is a guaranteed fee for availability or baseline service.
 Incentives are calculated on top of the retainer.
 Example: A proxy may receive Ksh 20,000/month retainer to guarantee availability, but can still earn incentives for volume/exceptional jobs.
 Statutory deductions: Withholding Tax (WHT) applied at the source where legally required.

2. Incentives (Performance-Based)
Formula:
Total Incentive = Standard + (Bonus Plus OR Whale)
2.1 mgmt_fee Calculation
mgmt_fee = agreed_service_percentage * total_service_value
 agreed_service_percentage is stored in the contracts table.
 total_service_value comes from the job order / trust account ledger.

2.2 Formula
 Standard Reward
standard = standard_rate * mgmt_fee
 Bonus Plus (target met + avg rating >=4.0/5)
bonus_plus = bonus_plus_rate * mgmt_fee
 Whale Bonus (flagged deal, exceptional threshold)
whale = whale_rate * mgmt_fee
 Mutual Exclusivity Rule: Whale and Bonus Plus cannot both apply to the same contract/job.

2.3 Cap Rule
final_incentive = min(total_incentive, cap_rate * mgmt_fee)
(default cap_rate = 20%)

2.4 Clawback Policy
 100% clawback if client/job cancels <=30 days.
 50% clawback if cancelation 31 - 90 days.
 0% clawback after 90 days.

3. Role-Specific Rates with Versioning
 External professional rates are stored in the roles table with:
 role_id
 standard_rate
 bonus_plus_rate
 whale_rate
 cap_rate
 effective_start_date
 effective_end_date
This ensures historical accuracy for past jobs and prevents recalculation errors when rates are updated.

4. Workflow Logic
 Job Completed --> contract_id + worker_id logged.
 Revenue Confirmed --> service fee received in trust account.
 Fetch Rates --> system checks role_id with rate versioning.
 Compute mgmt_fee --> agreed percentage x collected fee.
 System Calculation --> Standard + (Bonus Plus OR Whale), apply cap.
 Clawback Flag --> provisional for 90 days.
 Approval Step
 Normal = auto-approved.
 Whale or Cap triggered --> status = requires_approval.
 Payout Execution --> Retainer (if any) + Incentive  -  Clawbacks  -  WHT = Net Payout.
 Audit Log Entry --> all actions recorded.

5. Audit Log (With Reasons)
Stored as JSON blob in audit_log.
Example:
[
  {
    "timestamp": "2025-07-01T11:45:00Z",
    "user_id": 8,
    "action": "bonus_plus_awarded",
    "before": {"bonus_plus": 0},
    "after": {"bonus_plus": 4000},
    "reason": "Vendor achieved 10 verifications in June, policy 4.17"
  },
  {
    "timestamp": "2025-07-02T09:20:00Z",
    "user_id": 2,
    "action": "whale_flag_applied",
    "before": {"whale": 0},
    "after": {"whale": 12000},
    "reason": "Verified estate >100 units, policy 4.17"
  }
]

6. Status States
 pending --> awaiting calculation
 requires_approval --> Whale or Cap triggered
 approved --> Finance/CEO approved
 paid --> disbursed to vendor

7. Database (External Incentives Table)
 incentive_id
 contract_id
 worker_id
 mgmt_fee
 standard_amount
 bonus_plus_amount
 whale_amount
 total_before_cap
 cap_amount
 final_incentive
 clawback_status (none / 100% / 50%)
 clawback_amount
 status (pending / requires_approval / approved / paid)
 approved_by
 approved_at
 audit_log (JSON with reasons)

8. Dashboards
Vendor Dashboard:
 Retainer (if any)
 Service fee value (mgmt_fee)
 Incentives (Standard + Bonus Plus OR Whale)
 Clawback notices
 WHT deductions
 Net payout & status
Admin/Finance Dashboard:
 External payroll liability overview
 Pending approvals
 Clawback tracker
 Tax reports (WHT)
 Audit log viewer

✅ With the retainer clarification, your External Payroll is now:
 Crystal clear on how incentives stack with retainers.
 Symmetrical with internal payroll rules.
 Audit-proof with JSON logs & reasons.
 Governance-controlled (approval workflow).
 Scalable via role-based rates + versioning.

Petty Cash & OPEX Reserve (Operational Financial Control System)

1. Petty Cash Float
 Purpose: Covers small, day-to-day expenses where immediate digital payment may be impractical.
 Float Level: Fixed at Ksh 30,000.
 System Ledger: Balance tracked in petty_cash_wallet.
 Disbursement: Always digital (M-Pesa Till, company wallet, or bank transfer). Physical cash use is discouraged.
 Replenishment:
 Not automatic.
 Requires a weekly Replenishment Request from the Admin.
 Must include: attached receipts, categorized expenses, and closing balance confirmation.
 Reviewed and approved by the Finance Officer (Four-Eyes Principle).
 Once approved, funds move from OPEX Reserve --> Petty Cash Wallet to restore the float.

2. OPEX Reserve Account
 Purpose: Covers payroll, subscriptions, marketing, utilities, and other large operational expenses.
 Funding: Maintained in a separate bank account, funded by company revenue or investment capital.
 Segregation Rule: Absolutely separate from Client Trust Accounts.
 Balance Target: At least 3 months of forecasted OPEX.

3. Expense Classification & Urgency
 Planned Expenses: Predictable, scheduled costs (e.g., payroll, software, rent). Must be requested and pre-approved before disbursement.
 Urgent Expenses: Unexpected, time-sensitive costs that risk:
 Service continuity (e.g., server downtime).
 Property damage (e.g., emergency repair).
 Legal deadlines (e.g., last-minute court filing).
 May bypass pre-approval but must be ratified by the next authority level within 24 hours, with justification attached.

4. Multi-Tier Approval Matrix
Expense Range (Ksh)
Approval Authority
Source of Funds
0  -  5,000
Admin
Petty Cash
5,001  -  50,000
Finance Officer
Petty / OPEX
50,001+
CEO
OPEX Reserve
 System Routing: Approval matrix is hard-coded; requests are auto-routed to the correct dashboard.

5. Workflow & Controls
 Request --> Approval --> Disbursement --> Reconciliation
 System-Enforced Routing: Approval limits enforced by code.
 Digital-First Rule: Every disbursement must leave a digital transaction record.
 Audit Trail: Every action logged immutably.

6. Finance Dashboard (Live Financial Command Center)
 Budget vs Actual: Real-time spending by category (Payroll, Marketing, Tech, etc.).
 Automated Alerts: Notify when a category reaches 90% of its monthly budget.
 Urgent Spend Tracker: Logs urgent requests and their justifications.
 Replenishment Status: Shows pending vs approved petty cash requests.

7. Immutable Audit Log
Every transaction entry must include:
 timestamp
 user_id (who requested/approved)
 amount
 category (transport, payroll, marketing, etc.)
 type (petty | OPEX | urgent | planned)
 approval_level (Admin | Finance | CEO)
 before_balance, after_balance
 reason (business justification)
 related_document_url (link to scanned receipt/invoice)

✅ With this, you now have a digital-first, role-based, audit-ready petty cash and OPEX reserve system that is:
 CFO-friendly (strong financial controls).
 Developer-friendly (clearly defined fields and workflows).
 Scalable (works for small or large teams).

Secure Living: Enhanced Managerial Finance Dashboard Specifications
Objective: To provide managers with a proactive, intelligent, and efficient interface for overseeing all company finances, from petty cash to strategic OPEX management.
1. Petty Cash Float Widget
 Feature: A dashboard widget showing the current balance and a scrollable list of the "Last 5 Transactions."
 Fields Displayed: Date, Amount, Category, Vendor/Recipient.
 Managerial Action: A "Flag for Review" button next to each transaction. When clicked, it prompts for a reason and notifies the Finance Officer.
 Why it Matters: Provides at-a-glance oversight to prevent misuse and catch errors immediately.
2. OPEX Runway Visualizer
 Feature: A dynamic gauge or progress bar for the "Months of Runway" metric.
 Visual Indicators:
 Green (> 4 months): "Healthy"
 Yellow (2 - 4 months): "Monitor"
 Red (< 2 months): "Critical"
 Managerial Action: The system automatically sends an email and dashboard alert to the CEO and Finance Officer when the status hits "Red". The alert includes a direct link to the financial forecast and funding history.
 Why it Matters: Offers an instant, intuitive understanding of the company's financial health and triggers strategic action.
3. Urgent Spend Analyzer
 Feature: A system-calculated metric: (Total Urgent Spend / Total OPEX Spend) * 100
 Dashboard Display: A percentage value with a trend arrow (e.g., ↑ 18%).
 Managerial Action: If the ratio exceeds a configurable threshold (default: 15%), the system flags it. The CFO/CEO can then generate a "Process Review" report to analyze the root causes of reactive spending. Define conditions where supervisor approval is mandatory (e.g., cost > KES 50,000, repeat issue within 30 days, compliance flag)
 Why it Matters: Shifts focus from micromanaging single expenses to improving overall operational planning and efficiency.
4. Approval Delegation Module
 Feature: An "Approval Delegation" section in the CEO's dashboard.
 Functionality:
 CEO selects a delegate (e.g., Finance Officer), sets a budget limit (e.g., up to Ksh 70,000) and an expiry date/time.
 The system notifies the delegate and logs the delegation event in the audit trail.
 All approvals made by the delegate are clearly marked as "Approved by [Delegate] on behalf of [CEO]".
 Managerial Action: Prevents bureaucratic delays during travel or leave while maintaining a clear, auditable chain of responsibility.
 Why it Matters: Balances control with operational agility.
5. Forecast vs. Actual Trend Analysis
 Feature: On the budget vs. actual chart, add a trend line projecting future spend based on the current month's burn rate.
 Visualization: A dotted line extending beyond the current date.
 Managerial Action: Allows managers to see if they are on track to exceed budget before the 90% threshold alert. This enables proactive adjustments to spending.
 Why it Matters: Moves management from reactive to predictive, saving money and improving fiscal discipline.
6. One-Click Audit Pack Generator
 Feature: A "Reports" section with buttons for pre-configured "Audit Packs".
 Standard Reports:
 Monthly Finance Pack: For board meetings. Summarizes income, OPEX, budget variances, and key metrics.
 Annual Audit Trail: A comprehensive log for external accountants, filterable by date and category.
 Urgent Spend Justification Report: Lists all urgent expenses with their reasons and approvers for a given period.
 Managerial Action: Managers click a button to generate and download a PDF or CSV report instantly.
 Why it Matters: Drastically reduces administrative overhead for compliance and reporting, saving countless hours.
Implementation Summary for Developers:
Feature
Technical Requirement

Widget on Petty Cash dashboard tile. Query transactions table, sort by timestamp DESC, limit 5.

opex_balance, avg_monthly_opex

type=urgent, amount, timestamp

delegator_id, delegate_id, max_amount, expires_at

historical_spend, current_burn_rate

This enhanced dashboard provides managers with predictive insights, proactive alerts, and streamlined controls, embodying the principle that good financial management software doesn't just report the past -- it helps you intelligently manage the future.

🔹 Implementation Strategy: RBAC (Role-Based Access Control)
Tables Required:
 roles --> ceo, finance_manager, ops_manager, marketing_manager, field_staff.
 permissions --> defines accessible dashboards, data, and actions.
 user_roles --> links users to their roles.
Example:
 accounts_payable table visible to ceo + finance_manager, hidden from marketing_manager and field_staff.
 maintenance_response_time_alerts visible to ops_manager + ceo, hidden from finance/marketing.

Admin Configuration Rules


Supervision Assignment Rule:
- `Task-Type/Inspection/Emergency` --> Auto-assign `Supervision-Level/Supervisor-Managed`
- `Task-Type/Leasing/Commercial-Lease` --> Auto-assign `Supervision-Level/Supervisor-Approval`
- `Priority-Level/VIP-Client` --> Auto-assign `Supervision-Level/Supervisor-Managed`

Approval Workflow Mapping:
- `Task-Type/Leasing/*` + `rent > 100,000` --> `Approval-Workflow/Supervisor-Approval`
- `Task-Type/Compliance/Financial-Audit` --> `Approval-Workflow/Dual-Approval`
- `Priority-Level/Emergency` --> `Approval-Workflow/Auto-Approved` (with post-facto review)

So should I paste this "Complete Role-Based Access & Dashboard Design with Taxonomy Configuration" or "Complete Role-Based Access & Dashboard Design with Strategic Enhancements" I am confused
We have two versions: one with strategic enhancements and one with taxonomy configuration. The taxonomy configuration version is more detailed and includes the strategic enhancements as well, but with an added layer of taxonomy.
If the user is looking for a more comprehensive system that includes categorization and classification of entities (like properties, services, etc.) for better management and role assignments, then the taxonomy version is the way to go.
However, if the user does not need the complexity of taxonomy and wants a more straightforward role-based system with the strategic enhancements (like badges, crisis mode, bias mitigation), then the strategic enhancements version is sufficient.
Given that the user has not expressed a need for taxonomy and the previous discussions were about roles, permissions, and strategic enhancements, I would recommend the "Complete Role-Based Access & Dashboard Design with Strategic Enhancements" for now.
But if the user is planning to have a complex structure of properties, services, and other entities that require categorization, then the taxonomy version would be more suitable.
Let me know if you have any specific requirements that would help decide.
Use "Complete Role-Based Access & Dashboard Design with Taxonomy Configuration" - it's the most comprehensive version that includes all the strategic enhancements plus the taxonomy system.

✅ This Full Implementation Plan ties the strategic design (role dashboards + branch model) with practical engineering execution (DB, middleware, frontend, alerts, rollout).
Secure Living  --  Full Implementation Plan (Complete, Developer-Ready)
Purpose: single master guide for devs that covers architecture, DB, RBAC, API & middleware, frontend contracts, dashboards per role, alerts, reporting, security, ops, testing, rollout phases, and deliverables. Includes branch (branch_id) enforcement everywhere and CEO branch override.

1. Core concept  --  Branch-based architecture (recap)
 Everything is branch-scoped. branch_id tags every transactional record.
 Branch is flexible: city, region, property, brand, or unit.
 permissions table governs which branches a role or user can access.
 CEO has global visibility; can use Branch Selector to override/aggregate.

2. Data Model & Schema (key tables)
 branches(id,name,slug,description,meta...,created_at)
 users(id,email,name,primary_branch_id,created_at)
 roles(id,role_name)
 user_roles(id,user_id,role_id,created_at)
 permissions(id,role_id,branch_id)  --  core: role --> branch mapping
 properties(...,branch_id,...), leases(...,branch_id,...)
 maintenance_requests(...,branch_id,property_id,status,...)
 transactions(...,branch_id,amount,category,...)
 vendors, branch_vendors(vendor_id,branch_id)
 audit_logs(id,user_id,branch_id,endpoint,action,payload_hash,created_at) (append-only)
 notifications(id,branch_id,type,priority,payload,ack_by,ack_at,created_at)
Indexes & notes
 Index branch_id on all transactional tables; composite (branch_id, created_at) where needed.
 Consider materialized views for expensive aggregate KPIs per branch.

3. RBAC & Permissions semantics
 permissions rows define the allowed branch_id set for roles.
 Effective branches for a user are computed through their user_roles --> join to permissions.
 Optionally support user_permissions for user-level exceptions (if needed).
 role_name='ceo' treated as global (permissions could store NULL branch_id for global).
Effective-branch lookup example
SELECT DISTINCT p.branch_id
FROM user_roles ur
JOIN permissions p ON p.role_id = ur.role_id
WHERE ur.user_id = :user_id;

4. Authentication & Identity management (new)
 Support OAuth2 / SSO (Google, Microsoft) for enterprise convenience.
 Enforce Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for high-privilege roles (CEO, Finance).
 Secure password flow: salted hashing (bcrypt/argon2), password reset tokens, rate limits.
 Sessions & tokens: short-lived access tokens, refresh tokens, revoke list.
 Provisioning: Admin UI to invite users, assign roles/branches.

5. Backend  --  Middleware & branch enforcement (critical)
 Auth middleware:
 Identify user from auth token.
 Resolve user roles and allowed branch_id list.
 For non-CEO: set req.branchFilter = allowedBranches (or single branch if assigned).
 For CEO: req.branchFilter = null (global) unless ?branch_id=X specified.
 Apply branchFilter at ORM/query layer globally (use ORM scopes or query wrapper).
 Log every request into audit_logs with user_id + effective branches used.
Pseudocode
if (user.role == 'ceo') {
  req.branchFilter = req.query.branch_id ? [bid] : null;
} else {
  const allowed = getAllowedBranchesForUser(user.id);
  if (req.query.branch_id && !allowed.includes(bid)) reject;
  req.branchFilter = req.query.branch_id ? [bid] : allowed;
}

6. Frontend contracts & behavior
 CEO: Branch Selector dropdown ("All branches" + list).
 "All branches" --> no branch_id parameter (backend returns global).
 Specific branch --> send ?branch_id=X on API calls (or set global app-state branch param).
 Managers / Staff: no branch selector; their allowed branch(s) set in auth response and enforced by frontend state + backend middleware.
 Export to CSV: UI triggers same API (?format=csv), backend enforces branch filter and handles large exports via background jobs.
 UI contract: All responses include meta: { effective_branch_filter: [...], pagination }.

7. Dashboards & role views (branch-aware)
 CEO  -  Command Center (Global + per-branch)
 CEO Summary widget (Runway, Lead-to-Lease %, Overall NPS, Revenue split).
 Branch selector + cross-branch comparison charts.
 Alerts center, Roadmap tracking (Gantt + OKRs).
 Finance Manager
 Petty cash widget (last 5 txns + flag).
 OPEX runway visualizer (traffic light).
 Vendor aging (clickable vendor drilldowns: contact, invoices, branch links).
 In-dashboard approvals for replenishments; accounts payable aging per branch.
 Operations Manager
 CRM funnel and LQS per branch; CEI & NPS per community/branch.
 Maintenance metrics + Predictive alerting on response time drift.
 Task assignments & workload.
 Marketing Manager
 Leads by source per branch; CPL by channel; campaign ROI; funnel contribution per channel; authority metrics.
 Field Staff
 Mobile-first task list, status updates (Done/Pending/Blocked), no KPIs.
 Cross-role features
 Export to CSV on every table.
 Drilldowns from KPI to transactions/items.

8. Alerts engine & background jobs (new)
 Scheduler (cron/worker) computes KPIs per branch and creates alerts:
 Runway: months_of_runway < 2 --> critical; <4 --> warning.
 Urgent Spend Ratio > threshold (default 15%).
 CEI decline > configured threshold.
 LQS sudden drop (>20% vs previous period).
 Maintenance response drift > 1.25x baseline.
 Notifications persisted in notifications table; push channels: in-app + email + optional SMS/WhatsApp for critical alerts.
 Alerts must include suggested actions and direct links to relevant dashboards/reports.

9. Finance controls & workflows (recap + integrated)
 Petty cash workflow: Admin submits replenishment (with receipts) --> Finance Officer approves (Four-eyes) --> transfer OPEX --> update petty_cash_wallet.
 Multi-tier approval matrix enforced by middleware + UI:
 0 - 5k --> Admin (petty)
 5,001 - 50k --> Finance Officer
 50,001+ --> CEO (or delegated)
 Approval Delegation feature: CEO can delegate for a time-bound period; logged in audit trail.
 Immutable audit logs for all approvals and money movements.

10. Audit & reporting features (enhanced)
 Audit Pack generator: Monthly Finance Pack, Annual Audit Trail, Urgent Spend Justification, Petty Cash Reconciliation  --  one-click PDF/CSV.  Report Generator is tool for all service provider, professionals and Secure Living Staff, similar to the quotation.
 Append-only audit_logs with payload_hash for tamper-evidence.
 Export endpoints use the same branch filters and permission checks.
 Reporting: prebuilt Branch Performance Pack (benchmarks across branches).

11. Security & compliance (new)
 TLS everywhere, HSTS, secure cookies, CSP headers.
 Protect endpoints accepting branch_id  --  validate against user permissions.
 Rate limit APIs and protect admin endpoints with stricter MFA & IP allowlists.
 Immutable audit logs retention policy (7+ years for finance).
 Penetration testing prior to production go-live.
 Logging & monitoring (ELK/Datadog), alerts for repeated 403s or suspicious access.





11b. Step-Up Authentication for Critical Decisions
Purpose:
Adds an extra verification layer for high-stakes actions (finance approvals, fund transfers, refunds, legal commitments, role changes). Prevents impersonation, blame-shifting, and enforces personal accountability.

Core Features
 Dual Authentication Layers
:: Standard login credentials for daily use.
:: Step-Up authentication (OTP, PIN, biometric, or secondary password) required for critical decisions.
 Configurable Thresholds (Super Admin-controlled)
:: Super Admin sets which actions require Step-Up (e.g., payouts > KES 50,000).
:: Thresholds differ by role: Branch Manager <= KES 100k, Executive >= KES 500k, Super Admin = unlimited.
 Immutable Audit Logs
:: Every Step-Up event is logged with:
 User ID
 Action Type (finance_approval, payout_release, role_change, etc.)
 Auth Method
 Timestamp
 Outcome (success/failure)
:: Non-editable, non-deletable, tamper-proof.
 Fail-Safe & Alerts
:: Failed Step-Up attempts trigger Super Admin alerts.
:: Automatic lockout/escalation after multiple failures.
:: Users receive confirmation notices for every Step-Up action.

Delegated Step-Up Rights (Assistant Super Admins)
 Temporary Delegation
:: Super Admin can grant another user (e.g., Assistant Super Admin) Step-Up rights for a defined time period.
:: Example: "Approve financial actions up to KES 200,000 for 7 days."
 Scope-Based Delegation
:: Delegation limited to:
 Action Type (only finance approvals, not role changes).
 Branch/Region (e.g., Nairobi West only).
 Approval Limit (e.g., <= KES 200,000).
 Non-Transferable
:: Delegate cannot re-delegate their Step-Up rights.
:: System enforces this restriction automatically.
 Audit Trail Transparency
:: Logs clearly show: "Action approved by Assistant Super Admin (delegated rights from [Super Admin ID])."
:: Delegation event itself is logged (who delegated, scope, time, expiry).

Example Use Case
 You (Super Admin) are traveling.
 You delegate Step-Up rights to your assistant for Nairobi West Branch only and transactions <= KES 200,000.
 Assistant approves a landlord payout with their own Step-Up password.
 System logs:
:: "Delegation: Super Admin --> Assistant (scope: Nairobi_West, <=KES 200k, valid 7 days)."
:: "Approval: Assistant Super Admin approved payout 150,000 KES under delegated rights."

Technical Implementation Notes
 Delegation Table (new):
CREATE TABLE delegated_rights (
  id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
  delegator_id INT NOT NULL,
  delegate_id INT NOT NULL,
  action_type TEXT,
  branch_id INT,
  approval_limit NUMERIC,
  valid_from TIMESTAMP,
  valid_until TIMESTAMP,
  created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT now()
);
 Middleware check: before executing Step-Up action, verify if user is:
:: Super Admin OR
:: Delegate with valid, unexpired rights + within scope.
 Auto-expiry: system removes rights after valid_until.

   3) DB migration SQL (starter)
   Run these in your DB migration tool (example uses Postgres syntax). Adjust types/names to your stack.
   -- 1) branches
   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS branches (
     id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
     name TEXT NOT NULL,
     slug TEXT UNIQUE,
     description TEXT,
     meta JSONB DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
     created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT now()
   );
   
   -- 2) roles
   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS roles (
     id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
     role_name TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
     created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT now()
   );
   
   -- 3) users (add primary_branch_id nullable)
   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (
     id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
     email TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
     name TEXT,
     primary_branch_id INT REFERENCES branches(id),
     password_hash TEXT, -- for local auth
     created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT now()
   );
   
   -- 4) user_roles
   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS user_roles (
     id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
     user_id INT REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
     role_id INT REFERENCES roles(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
     created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT now()
   );
   
   -- 5) permissions (role -> branch mapping)
   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS permissions (
     id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
     role_id INT REFERENCES roles(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
     branch_id INT REFERENCES branches(id),
     created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT now(),
     UNIQUE(role_id, branch_id)
   );
   
   -- 6) audit_logs (append-only)
   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS audit_logs (
     id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
     user_id INT,
     branch_id INT,
     endpoint TEXT,
     action TEXT,
     payload_hash TEXT,
     meta JSONB DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
     created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT now()
   );
   -- optionally prevent DELETE/UPDATE via DB policies or rely on app-level discipline
   
   -- 7) notifications table (engine scaffold)
   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS notifications (
     id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
     branch_id INT,
     type TEXT NOT NULL,
     priority TEXT NOT NULL,
     payload JSONB NOT NULL,
     acknowledged_by INT,
     acknowledged_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE,
     created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT now()
   );
   
   -- 8) Add branch_id to transactional tables (example)
   ALTER TABLE properties ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS branch_id INT REFERENCES branches(id);
   ALTER TABLE maintenance_requests ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS branch_id INT REFERENCES branches(id);
   ALTER TABLE transactions ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS branch_id INT REFERENCES branches(id);
   ALTER TABLE leases ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS branch_id INT REFERENCES branches(id);
   
   -- 9) vendors & branch_vendors
   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS vendors (
     id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
     name TEXT NOT NULL,
     contact_phone TEXT,
     contact_email TEXT,
     created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT now()
   );
   
   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS branch_vendors (
     id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
     vendor_id INT REFERENCES vendors(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
     branch_id INT REFERENCES branches(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
     created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT now(),
     UNIQUE(vendor_id, branch_id)
   );
   
   -- Indexes
   CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_properties_branch ON properties(branch_id);
   CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_mt_requests_branch ON maintenance_requests(branch_id);
   CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_transactions_branch ON transactions(branch_id);
   
   4) Express.js middleware starter (branch enforcement + audit log)
   This is a minimal, secure pattern. Adapt to your stack (Django, Rails, etc.) as needed.
   // branchAuthMiddleware.js
   // Assumes: getUserFromToken, getAllowedBranchesForUser, logAudit
   
   async function branchAuthMiddleware(req, res, next) {
     try {
       const token = req.headers.authorization?.split(' ')[1];
       if (!token) return res.status(401).json({error: 'Missing token'});
   
       const user = await getUserFromToken(token); // implement secure token validation
       if (!user) return res.status(401).json({error: 'Invalid token'});
   
       req.user = user;
   
       // fetch allowed branches (cache this short-term)
       const allowedBranches = await getAllowedBranchesForUser(user.id); // returns [] or [1,2]
   
       // CEO role handling (global)
       const isCeo = user.roles?.includes('ceo') || user.is_superuser;
   
       if (isCeo) {
         if (req.query.branch_id) {
           const bid = parseInt(req.query.branch_id, 10);
           // optional: validate that branch exists
           req.branchFilter = [bid];
         } else {
           req.branchFilter = null; // null = no branch filter (global)
         }
       } else {
         // non-CEO: must have at least one allowed branch
         if (!allowedBranches || allowedBranches.length === 0) {
           return res.status(403).json({error: 'No branch access'});
         }
         if (req.query.branch_id) {
           const bid = parseInt(req.query.branch_id, 10);
           if (!allowedBranches.includes(bid)) {
             return res.status(403).json({error: 'Forbidden for branch'});
           }
           req.branchFilter = [bid];
         } else {
           req.branchFilter = allowedBranches; // array of allowed branches
         }
       }
   
       // Log the request in audit table (non-blocking)
       logAudit({
         user_id: user.id,
         branch_ids: req.branchFilter,
         endpoint: req.originalUrl,
         action: `${req.method}`,
         meta: { ip: req.ip, userAgent: req.headers['user-agent'] }
       }).catch(e => console.error('Audit log failed', e));
   
       next();
     } catch (err) {
       console.error('Auth middleware error', err);
       return res.status(500).json({error: 'Server error'});
     }
   }
   
   module.exports = branchAuthMiddleware;
   Developer notes
 Implement getUserFromToken to validate JWTs and return roles (and optionally allowed branches cached).
 Implement getAllowedBranchesForUser to query the user_roles --> permissions join. Cache result for short TTL (e.g., 60 - 300s).
 Use ORM-level scopes (Sequelize, TypeORM, Django QuerySet) to automatically apply req.branchFilter to all queries. Prefer server-side enforcement over trusting the front end.

🛡️ Advanced Safeguards
Finance Hire Timeline
 Bring on a Fractional CFO or Finance Manager early.
 Role: oversight, analysis, bank relationships, lead monthly deep dives.
 Not data entry  --  system automation handles that.
Bank & API Selection
 Choose escrow bank partners based on API reliability, reporting depth, and openness to fintech integration.
 Conduct technical due diligence before onboarding.
Burn Runway Standardization
 Define clearly what counts as "Monthly Burn."
 Must be consistent (e.g., operational costs only, or ops + planned CapEx).
 This ensures alerts are meaningful.
Disaster Recovery Plan
 If escrow reconciliation fails, system triggers automatic temporary suspension of fund releases.
 Manual SOP: freeze payouts, investigate discrepancy, restore only after reconciliation clears.
 Protects both clients and company reputation.

Examples:

 Tenant late rent = cash flow disruption highlighted. Late payment the system will still charge tenants, but distribution depends on landlord type.

🌍 Benefits
 For Management: Always informed, with early-warning alarms.

Anticipating Change & Adaptability Strategy
Change is inevitable, and Secure Living must not only respond but also anticipate and capitalize on it. This strategy formalizes how we sense, interpret, and act on external and internal change drivers  --  while measuring how fast and effectively we adapt.

1️⃣ Sensing Infrastructure --> The Early Warning System
 Leading Indicators Dashboard: Beyond lagging metrics like revenue, Secure Living tracks leading signals (e.g., spikes in Airbnb inquiries, escrow sign-ups tagged "construction oversight").
 Qualitative Mining: Structured cancellation data and NPS feedback provide early clues about dissatisfaction, feature demand, or geographic opportunities.
 Formal Quarterly Trends Report:
 Resourcing: Ownership must be clearly assigned (e.g., Product Manager or Fractional CFO) with dedicated time to compile the report.
 Review: A sacred, non-negotiable leadership session is held quarterly to discuss findings and decide actions.
 Structure: Report includes (1) findings, (2) recommended actions, (3) responsible owner, (4) timeline.
 Output Example: "Trend: VR inspection tools maturing --> Action: Allocate 5 dev-days in Q3 to prototype Vendor X integration."

2️⃣ Adaptive Culture --> The Human Operating System
 Hire for Flexibility: Recruit staff/Professionals with curiosity, adaptability, and problem-solving capacity.
 Continuous Training: Budget for future skills (software, regulation, agile methods).
 Frontline Empowerment Protocol: Professionals and staff can escalate issues through:
 An "Escalate Issue" button in their dashboard.
 A dedicated Slack/Teams channel monitored daily.
 A weekly review call for unresolved issues.
 Pressure Testing: Run simulations (e.g., a Professional submits a fake issue). Measure response time and ease of use. Goal = psychological safety, not bureaucracy.
 Quarterly "What If?" Workshops: Scenario planning with deep stress tests, e.g.:
 "What if Kenyan rental income is mandated through a central tax Action Panel?"
 "What if demand for Airbnb triples in 6 months?"
These sessions train the team to anticipate unlikely but high-impact events.

3️⃣ Flexible Operational & Technical Architecture
 API-First Design: Every major component (escrow, Professional management, reporting) is API-driven, allowing easy swaps and integrations.
 Modular Services: Treat Management, Projects, and Concierge as plug-and-play. New modules (e.g., Property Insurance) can be piloted or retired without disrupting the core.
 Innovation Sprints: Allocate ~20% of developer time to prototypes, integrations, or clearing technical debt.
 Leadership Protection: This time must be vigorously defended  --  it is not optional, but essential to long-term health.

4️⃣ Measuring Adaptability --> Speed to Response
 Metric Definition: Track "Speed to Response" = time from first signal (e.g., Quarterly Trends Report entry) to full deployment.
 Full Deployment Criteria: Clearly defined as:
 A feature launched to 100% of target users or
 Processing >=95% of intended transactions without critical errors.
 Targets: Aim to reduce average response cycle by 20% year-on-year.
 Visibility: Logged in Quarterly Trends Reports to track historical progress.

🌍 Outcomes
 For Clients: Confidence in a platform that keeps evolving (new services, languages, geographies).
 For Staff/Professionals: Empowered to escalate and adapt quickly, without fear of bureaucracy.
 For Secure Living: A living system that learns, adapts, and responds faster than competitors  --  embedding resilience directly into the company's DNA.

B. Wallet Data Flow Diagram
flowchart TD
    Tenant -->|Pays Rent via M-Pesa| Trust[Secure Living Trust Account]
    Trust -->|Auto-Deduct Fees| Wallet[Landlord Wallet Ledger]
    Wallet -->|Approve or Auto-Release| Bank[Landlord Bank/M-Pesa]
    Wallet -->|Hold Funds for Repairs/Projects| Professional[Professional Oversight Tasks]
    Professional -->|Approves Work| Release[Payment to Service Provider]

How It Works:
 Tenant pays rent --> Money lands in Trust Account.
 System auto-deducts fees (management fee, tax).
 Landlord Wallet updated  -  landlord sees real-time balance.
 If repairs/projects are pending --> funds held until Professional approves completion.
 Once verified --> payment released to service provider.

🔐 Tenant Secure Living Escrow System  --  Developer Guide 
🎯 Core Principle

Every payment is atomized into sub-accounts (escrow states) and tracked via immutable audit logs.
All operations are idempotent, concurrency-safe, and auditable.

1. Escrow Architecture
Escrow_Master (Escrow Master is the central ledger account where all funds first land before allocation to sub-wallets)

Definition: The central holding account/ledger where all incoming tenant payments first arrive.

Role: Temporary buffer before funds are split into sub-wallets.

Guarantee: Escrow_Master balance must always equal the sum of all sub-wallet balances (Rent_Escrow + Reserve_Escrow + Pending_Fees_Escrow + Dispute_Escrow + Deposit_Escrow).

2. Escrow Sub-Wallets (Ledger Design)

Rent_Escrow --> For base rent and fixed charges.

Reserve_Escrow --> Landlord's CapEx/maintenance savings.

Pending_Fees_Escrow --> Holds fines until cleared. Fines apply only to landlords who opt out of Advance Payout. For Advance landlords, Secure Living absorbs risk and forfeits fine revenue

Dispute_Escrow --> Holds disputed charges.

Deposit_Escrow --> Holds tenant security deposits.

Each sub-wallet = row in ledger tied to ledger_id (landlord) and source_id (tenant).

3. System Safeguards

Transaction Idempotency

All payment actions (allocation, refund, dispute) must use an idempotency key (txn_id).

If a retry occurs (network glitch, API timeout), the operation should return the original result instead of duplicating allocations.

Concurrency Control

Use database row-level locks or optimistic locking when updating balances.

Prevents race conditions (e.g., two simultaneous refunds) and ensures reconciliation never fails.

State Transitions (Transaction Lifecycle)
Transactions table must enforce valid state flows:

PENDING --> ALLOCATED --> RELEASED  
PENDING --> ALLOCATED --> IN_DISPUTE --> RESOLVED (RELEASED or REFUNDED)  
PENDING --> FAILED (error, insufficient funds)  


Illegal transitions (e.g., RELEASED --> PENDING) must be blocked.

These can be visualized in a state machine diagram.

Daily Escrow Reconciliation

Escrow_Master == SUM(all sub-wallet balances)  


If mismatch --> freeze payouts + alert CFO/CTO.

4. Database Schema (Key Tables)

Transactions Table
| txn_id | user_id | ledger_id | type | amount | sub_wallet | status | idempotency_key | timestamp |

Refunds Table
| refund_id | txn_id | reason_code | status | approver_id | payout_channel | timestamp |

Disputes Table
| dispute_id | txn_id | amount | reason | evidence | resolution | approver_id | timestamp |

Reserves Table
| reserve_id | ledger_id | balance | locked | usage_category | approver_id | timestamp |

5. API Endpoint Examples

Allocate Payment

POST /api/v1/payments/{txn_id}/allocate
Body: { amount, allocations: { rent: %, reserve: %, utilities: % } }
Headers: { Idempotency-Key: txn_id }


--> Splits funds from Escrow_Master into sub-wallets.

Open Dispute

POST /api/v1/disputes/{txn_id}/open
Body: { amount, reason, evidence_url }
Headers: { Idempotency-Key: dispute_id }


--> Moves funds into Dispute_Escrow until resolved.

Process Refund

POST /api/v1/refunds
Body: { txn_id, reason_code }
Headers: { Idempotency-Key: refund_id }


--> Validates original txn, processes refund to original channel.

6. KPIs for Monitoring

Escrow Accuracy Rate = 100% (non-negotiable).

Refund Resolution Time <= 5 days.

Dispute Closure Time <= 7 days.

Fine Collection Rate >= 80%.

Reserve Fund Growth tracked per landlord.






"Compassionate Advance Payouts" into Secure Living. It covers data model changes, business rules, APIs, UI flows, accounting entries, audit and reconciliation, controls, alerts, automated recovery, and tests. Developers can implement it directly or hand it to an engineering team.

1  --  Feature summary (what we're building)
A system that lets Secure Living advance landlord payouts from a company reserve when a reliable tenant (high TRI) notifies they'll pay late. The system:
 Verifies tenant eligibility (TRI threshold & communication flag).
 Creates an advance transaction to landlord from a designated company reserve escrow.
 Tracks the tenant debt (rent + optional fine) in their escrow sub-ledger.
 Auto-reconciles and recovers the advance when tenant pays.
 Logs full audit trail, approvals, and limits to prevent abuse.

2  --  High-level components
 Database changes: new tables/columns for Reserve Fund, Advances, CompassionRequests, TRI, Audit logs.
 Business rules engine: enforces eligibility, caps, limits, approvals.
 APIs: endpoints for creating requests, approving advances, tenant payments, reconciliation.
 UI components: Tenant Action Panel (request), Staff/Ops dashboard (approve/monitor), Landlord dashboard (notification).
 Accounting: automated journal entries for advances/recoveries.
 Monitoring/Alerts: anomaly detection, reserve low alerts, overdue advance alerts.
 Tests/DR: unit, integration, and reconciliation tests.

3  --  Data model (schema additions / key fields)
Below are example SQL-ish table definitions (adapt to your DB dialect).
-- Company reserve fund ledger (single row for fund; sub-ledgers optional)
CREATE TABLE company_reserve (
  id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
  balance_cents BIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  currency VARCHAR(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'KES',
  created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT now(),
  updated_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT now()
);

-- Advance requests made by staff or auto-requested by tenant
CREATE TABLE advance_requests (
  id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
  tenant_id UUID NOT NULL,
  landlord_id UUID NOT NULL,
  property_id UUID NOT NULL,
  lease_id UUID,
  rent_amount_cents BIGINT NOT NULL,
  fine_amount_cents BIGINT DEFAULT 0,
  advance_amount_cents BIGINT NOT NULL, -- rent + fine (or part of it)
  requested_by UUID, -- user who created the request (tenant or staff)
  requested_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT now(),
  tri_at_request INT, -- TRI value snapshot
  reason TEXT,
  status ENUM('pending','approved','rejected','paid_back','defaulted') DEFAULT 'pending',
  approved_by UUID,
  approved_at TIMESTAMP,
  payout_txn_id UUID, -- link to escrow payout transaction
  recovery_txn_id UUID, -- link to recovery transaction when tenant pays
  auto_recovery BOOLEAN DEFAULT TRUE,
  notes JSONB,
  created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT now(),
  updated_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT now()
);

-- Tenant sub-ledger row to represent tenant's outstanding obligations
CREATE TABLE tenant_obligations (
  id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
  tenant_id UUID NOT NULL,
  advance_request_id INT REFERENCES advance_requests(id),
  amount_due_cents BIGINT NOT NULL,
  due_date DATE,
  recovered_cents BIGINT DEFAULT 0,
  status ENUM('open','partially_recovered','recovered','written_off') DEFAULT 'open',
  created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT now(),
  updated_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT now()
);

-- Audit log for all sensitive actions
CREATE TABLE action_audit_log (
  id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
  entity_type VARCHAR(50),
  entity_id UUID,
  action VARCHAR(100),
  performed_by UUID,
  performed_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT now(),
  metadata JSONB
);
Also ensure your existing tenant, landlord, lease, escrow_transactions, and tri_scores tables have clear primary keys and references.

4  --  Business rules (logic)
 Eligibility
 Tenant TRI must be >= TRI_THRESHOLD (configurable, e.g., 80).
 Tenant must have communicated (a tenant-initiated request or a staff ticket marked as "tenant communicated").
 Tenant must not have more than X active advances in the past Y months (configurable, e.g., 1 advance per 12 months).
 Advance cap per tenant = min( rent_amount + fine, MAX_ADVANCE_AMOUNT ), e.g., no more than 1 month's rent or Ksh 100,000.
 Approval flow
 If advance_amount <= AUTO_APPROVAL_LIMIT (configurable), system auto-approves.
 If > AUTO_APPROVAL_LIMIT --> Ops Lead or Finance must manually approve in the dashboard.
 Approval writes to action_audit_log.
 Payout
 Once approved, create an escrow payout from company_reserve to landlord account and record payout_txn_id.
 Create tenant_obligations record to track owed amount.
 Notify landlord and tenant.
 Recovery
 When tenant pays rent (payment flows into escrow), system first allocates incoming funds to tenant_obligations (priority: outstanding advance recovery --> rent next).
 Once obligation amount_due is fully recovered, mark tenant_obligations.status = recovered and advance_requests.status = paid_back.
 Create accounting ledger entries: debit tenant escrow sub-ledger, credit company_reserve (recover), and record in recovery_txn_id.
 Defaults & write-offs
 If tenant fails to pay within DEFAULT_WINDOW (e.g., 60 days), mark advance_requests.status = defaulted, start recovery action (legal collection, deposit deduction, insurance claim where applicable).
 Write-off only with CFO approval and logged in audit.
 Compassion adjustments
 Optionally reduce or waive fine (one free compassionate waiver per tenant per 12 months) if landlord agrees. This must be recorded.
 Limits
 Company reserve must have min_buffer (e.g., 2 x average monthly payout)  --  if below, auto-reject advances and alert Finance.

5  --  API endpoints (examples)
Use REST or GraphQL. Example REST endpoints:
POST /api/advance-requests
Request body:
{
  "tenant_id": "...",
  "landlord_id": "...",
  "property_id": "...",
  "lease_id": "...",
  "rent_amount_cents": 400000,
  "fine_amount_cents": 20000,
  "requested_by": "...",
  "reason": "Delayed salary payment, tenant notified"
}

GET /api/advance-requests/{id}           -- view status & audit
POST /api/advance-requests/{id}/approve  -- finance or auto
POST /api/advance-requests/{id}/reject
POST /api/advance-requests/{id}/recover  -- triggered by payment reconciler
GET /api/company-reserve                 -- balance + min_buffer
POST /api/company-reserve/topup          -- (finance) add funds
API responses must include tri_at_request, eligibility_reasons when rejected, action_audit_log reference.

6  --  UI/UX flows
Tenant Action Panel
 Button: "I'll be late with rent" --> short form: expected delay days, message, evidence if needed.
 Confirmation pop-up: informs tenant of possible fine & recovery.
 Submits advance_request (status: pending).
Staff/Customer Support
 Ticket appears in Ops dashboard: shows tenant TRI, history, and suggested action (auto-approve or require approval).
 Ops can Approve (auto if criteria met) or Request more info, or Reject.
 Option: apply compassionate waiver (checkbox). If compassionate waiver used, must log landlord/ property manager consent or auto-notify property manager/landlord with approve button. If the advance is made the landlord, the fine should be directed to the property manager as part of the compensation in management.
Finance Dashboard
 Shows company reserve balance and list of pending/approved advances, overdue recoveries.
 Auto-approve rules visible; manual approvals require 2FA and record in action_audit_log.
Landlord Dashboard
 Notification of advance payout, with label "paid from Secure Living Reserve  --  tenant will repay" and link to tenant obligation.
 Monthly statement includes advance and recovery history.

7  --  Accounting & ledger entries (example)
Use cents to avoid float issues.
On advance payout:
 Debit: company_reserve (asset) --> decrease by advance_amount
 Credit: escrow_payouts_payable / or transfer out of company reserve to landlord bank
On tenant payment (recovery):
 Tenant payment hits tenant escrow sub-ledger (liability)
 System applies recovery:
 Debit: tenant_escrow_liability (reduce)
 Credit: company_reserve (increase)  --  recovered funds replace advance
 If fine applied:
 Fine receipts may be recorded as revenue as company policy allows (or as tenant-recovery revenue), shown in P&L appropriately.
All journal entries must be linked to advance_request.id and escrow_transaction.id. Ensure audit trails.

8  --  Reconciliation & daily jobs
 Daily reconciler checks:
 company_reserve.balance_cents vs sum of reserve-related escrow ledger.
 All advance_requests with status approved and payout_txn_id are reconciled to bank transactions.
 Recovery job runs on incoming tenant payments:
 For each payment, allocate to oldest tenant_obligations first (FIFO).
 Update tenant_obligations.recovered_cents and statuses.
 Alerts:
 Reserve below min_buffer --> email + SMS to CFO and CEO.
 Overdue advance (> DEFAULT_WINDOW) --> weekly escalation to legal + Ops.

9  --  Security, permissions & audit
 Only finance role can approve advances above AUTO_APPROVAL_LIMIT.
 Approvals must be 2FA for amounts > HIGH_APPROVAL_LIMIT.
 All actions recorded in action_audit_log with actor, timestamp, and metadata.
 Admin UI must show a tamper-evident audit trail and immutable references to supporting documents (photo receipts, tenant message).

10  --  UX & product considerations
 Make it human: show landlord a short summary and require opt-in for compassionate policy in onboarding (so they consent to advanced payouts rules).
 Transparency: On landlord invoices show a line: "Advanced payout: Ksh X on YYYY (tenant to repay)".
 Notifications: Real-time push/email to tenant/landlord when advance paid, when recovery applies, and when request is rejected.

11  --  Testing & QA
 Unit tests: business rules (eligibility, caps, TRI threshold).
 Integration tests: end-to-end flow (tenant requests --> auto-approve --> payout --> tenant pays --> recovery).
 Edge cases:
 Reserve insufficient --> reject advance.
 Tenant pays partial --> partial recovery, obligation open.
 Multiple advances in window --> reject if over limit.
 Reconciliation tests: simulate bank delays and mismatches; ensure freeze and alert behavior works.

12  --  Monitoring & metrics to expose
 advances requested / approved / defaulted
 Average time to recovery
 % advances recovered within 30 days
 Reserve utilization ratio (reserved / min_buffer)
 TRI distribution of tenants who request advances
 Landlord satisfaction for advanced payouts (post-survey)

13  --  Example pseudocode (simplified)
def create_advance_request(tenant_id, lease_id, rent_cents, fine_cents, reason, requested_by):
    tri = get_tri(tenant_id)
    if tri < TRI_THRESHOLD:
        return reject("TRI too low")
    if tenant_has_recent_advance(tenant_id):
        return reject("Recent advance exists")
    advance_amount = rent_cents + fine_cents
    if advance_amount > MAX_ADVANCE:
        return reject("Above max allowed")

    req = db.advance_requests.insert({...tri_at_request:tri, ...})
    if advance_amount <= AUTO_APPROVAL_LIMIT and reserve.balance >= advance_amount + min_buffer:
        approve_advance(req.id, auto=True)
    else:
        notify_finance(req.id)
    return req

def approve_advance(request_id, approver_id):
    req = db.advance_requests.get(request_id)
    if company_reserve.balance < req.advance_amount_cents + min_buffer:
        raise Exception("Insufficient reserve")
    payout_txn = escrow_api.payout_to_landlord(req.landlord_id, req.advance_amount_cents, metadata)
    db.create_tenant_obligation(req.tenant_id, req.id, req.advance_amount_cents, due_date = today + RECOVERY_WINDOW)
    db.advance_requests.update(id, status='approved', approved_by=approver_id, payout_txn_id=payout_txn.id)
    company_reserve.balance -= req.advance_amount_cents
    audit.log("approve_advance", request_id, approver_id)

14  --  Risk controls & anti-abuse mechanics
 One-time compassionate fine waiver per tenant per 12 months; recorded and visible to landlord.
 Auto-flag tenants that repeatedly request advances (use this to lower TRI).
 Reserve risk: keep dedicated contingency fund; disallow advances when reserve < min_buffer.
 Legal path documented for recovering defaults (deduct deposit, use insurance, legal notice).

15  --  Rollout plan for devs (milestones)
 Schema migration and unit tests.
 Build API endpoints and services for requests and approvals.
 Integrate with escrow transaction system for payouts & recoveries.
 Implement UI forms in Tenant Action Panel / Ops dashboard / Finance console.
 Build reconciliation & recovery background jobs.
 Security review & 2FA on approvals.
 End-to-end QA & pilot with a small landlord set.
 Full release, monitor metrics for 90 days.


1. New Sub-Wallet & State
 Advances_to_Landlords: This is a liability account for Secure Living. It tracks the total amount of rent you have paid out to landlords before receiving it from tenants.
 Fines_Income: This is an income account for Secure Living. When a fine is collected and the landlord is enrolled in the advance program, the money flows here instead of to the landlord.
2. New Workflow: "Advance Payment & Fine Collection"
Here is the new, integrated flow:

3. Configuration & Rules
 Landlord Enrollment: This must be an explicit opt-in setting in the landlord's profile (landlord.advance_program_enabled = true/false).
 Advance Limits: To manage risk, set a maximum advance amount per landlord based on their history (e.g., "up to 2 months' rent").
 Repayment Trigger: The advance is automatically "repaid" when the tenant's late payment finally arrives. That payment goes into the Advances_to_Landlords sub-wallet to reduce the liability, not to the landlord.
4. API & Data Model Adjustments
 New Field in Transactions table: advance_repayment_flag (boolean). This marks a tenant payment that is being used to repay an advance.
 New Table landlord_advances:
ledger_id
advance_balance
credit_limit
is_enabled
 New Table advance_payments:
| payment_id | ledger_id | amount | status | repaid_by_txn_id | timestamp |
5. Enhanced Safeguards & KPIs
 New KPI: Advance Default Rate: The percentage of advanced funds that are not repaid by tenants within 90 days. This measures the risk of the program.
 Safeguard: If a landlord's advance_balance exceeds 90% of their credit_limit, the system should automatically stop issuing new advances and alert the Ops team.
 Transparency: Landlords enrolled in the program must have a clear dashboard showing:
 Total advances received
 Their remaining advance limit
 Late fees collected on their behalf (that they are forfeiting)
1. Two Landlord Enrollment Paths
Option A: Standard Management
 Features:
 Landlord chooses:
 Reserve % allocation (e.g., 5 - 10%).
 Fine type (Flat Ksh OR % of rent).
 Grace period (e.g., 3 - 7 days).
 Refund rules.
 Flow: Tenant late --> Landlord payout delayed until tenant pays --> Fine collected --> Fine split according to agreed terms (usually Secure Living retains, but landlord knows upfront). If tenants pay late, fines are collected and shared (e.g., 70% landlord, 30% management). This keeps them patient while still giving Secure Living an incentive.

Option B: Advance Payout Guarantee (APG)
 Features:
 Landlord does not choose fine structure.
 Secure Living guarantees 100% rent payout on due date (Reserve + Ops Wallet logic).
 All late fines automatically belong to Secure Living (compensating for advance risk).
 Flow: Tenant late --> Landlord still gets full rent on time from Reserve/Ops Wallet --> Fine charged to tenant --> Fine sent 100% to Secure Living. 

2. Onboarding Checklist Adjustment
When landlord signs up:
🔹 Step 1 --> Select Management Type
 ⬜ Standard Management (choose Reserve %, Fine type, Grace period).
 ⬜ Advance Payout Guarantee (Secure Living guarantees rent, no fine options).
🔹 Step 2 --> Configure Refund & Dispute Rules (applies to both).
🔹 Step 3 --> Accept Escrow & Compliance Policy (non-editable)
