Dispute Mediation Protocols
Unlock dispute tasks
Professional_Technical
Maintenance Escrow Standards
Unlock job board
 User cannot access core features until orientation is passed.
 System enforces task gating: locked features show 🔒 until required training completed.

Tier 5: Full Access Grant (Verified Client)
 User passes orientation.
 System issues final Set Password link.
 Status = verified_active.
 Role-based permissions + risk score initialized.

 🔹 Internal HR Lifecycle (Staff & Contractors)
Hiring & Account Creation
 HR/Admin creates account.
 Step-Up Auth required.
 Sensitive roles need dual approval (HR + Senior Admin).
 Probation auto-applied (90 days default).
Probation
 Probation flag 🔶 on dashboards.
 Restricted permissions (view-only on some features).
 Probation ends --> HR must confirm/extend/terminate.
Training Integration
 Role-specific training auto-assigned.
 Completion mandatory before unlocking sensitive workflows.
Contractor Accounts
 Strict start/end date.
 Auto-disable at expiry (midnight).
 Grace period optional (e.g., 24h for task closure).
Termination
 Triggered via Termination Workflow (Step-Up required).
 Immediate login suspension.
 Active tasks auto-reassigned.
 Escrow/finance rights revoked instantly.
 Immutable audit logs created.
 Exit Report auto-generated.

4. 🔹 Permission Matrix
A granular control system defines:
permission_matrix (
  user_type VARCHAR,
  action VARCHAR,                 -- e.g., approve_payout, assign_delegate
  min_status ENUM('probation','active','verified_active'),
  probation_restriction BOOLEAN DEFAULT false,
  requires_step_up BOOLEAN DEFAULT false
)
🔎 Examples
 Staff_Finance (probation): view only, no payouts.
 Landlord_Individual: can approve payouts, but Step-Up required > KES 100,000.
 Diaspora_Premium: video KYC required for high-value withdrawals.

5. 🔹 Risk Scoring Integration
Every user is continuously evaluated on risk dimensions:
risk_scores (
  user_id UUID,
  risk_type ENUM('financial','operational','compliance'),
  score INTEGER,                   -- 0 to 100
  last_updated TIMESTAMP
)
 Financial Risk --> repeated late payments, chargebacks.
 Operational Risk --> missed SLAs, incomplete tasks.
 Compliance Risk --> invalid license, fake docs, AML red flags.
⚡ Automated Reactions:
 Score > 70 --> Step-Up required for sensitive actions.
 Score > 90 --> Auto-suspension & Compliance review triggered.

6. 🔹 Delegation & Management Models
Default Mode: Managed by Secure Living
 Properties assigned manager_type = secure_living.
 All payouts, inspections, and disputes handled by Secure Living staff.
Self-Management Option
 Landlord may switch to manager_type = self_manage.
 Can manage themselves (DIY) or assign a delegate.
Delegation Rules
 Delegates must pass KYC.
 Delegation scopes:

7. 🔹 Security Overlays
 Step-Up Authentication
 Required for role creation, payouts, delegation, termination.
 Immutable Audit Logs
audit_logs (
  log_id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
  action_type VARCHAR,
  performed_by UUID,
  target_user UUID,
  justification TEXT,
  ip_address VARCHAR,
  timestamp TIMESTAMP
)
 Compliance Reporting
 One-click PDF packs for AML/CFT audits.
 Suspicious Pattern Alerts
 Mass terminations.
 Multiple failed payouts.
 Sudden delegation chain expansions.

8. 🔹 Super Admin Safeguards
Super Admin can impersonate any role but must comply with strict safeguards:
def super_admin_override(action, target_user, justification):
    require_step_up_auth()
    if action in ['finance_transfer','role_change','termination']:
        require_second_admin_approval_if_high_risk()
    log_override_with_justification()
    trigger_compliance_alert_if_needed()
 Always requires OTP or biometric (Step-Up).
 For financial/security actions --> dual approval enforced.
 Overrides generate automatic compliance alerts.

9. 🔹 Example End-to-End Workflows
Tenant --> Verified Client
 Tenant applies via portal.
 Admin promotes --> uploads ID + Lease.
 Orientation: Escrow Rent Training.
 Gets login --> dashboard unlocked.
Landlord (Self-Manage with Delegate)
 Landlord applies --> selects self-manage.
 Approves delegate (via OTP).
 Delegate capped payout authority (KES 50,000).
 Payout > 100K --> requires landlord OTP.
Diaspora Landlord
 Applies from abroad.
 Embassy-certified docs uploaded.
 Bank validated via micro-deposit.
 Orientation via video call.
 Dashboard access --> can monitor remotely, delegate locally.
Contractor Lifecycle
 HR sets Jan - Mar contract.
 Auto-disable Apr 1 midnight.
 Active tasks reassigned.
 Exit report sent to HR.

10. 🔹 UI/UX Hooks
 Probation badge 🔶 for new hires.
 Verified badge 🎓 for clients post-orientation.
 Locked tasks show 🔒 tooltip.
 Delegation tree diagram with expiry countdown.
 Risk score meter (Green --> Amber --> Red).
 Offboarding popup requiring justification + audit log.

✅ Benefits
 Zero spam/fraud: Public portal isolated from main system.
 Controlled growth: Admin-assisted promotion only.
 End-to-end accountability: Immutable logs + Step-Up enforcement.
 Flexible management: Default Secure Living handling + optional self-manage.
 Adaptive risk control: Scores dynamically adjust permissions.
 Super admin transparency: All overrides logged with dual control.



🛡️ Section XX: Super Admin & Oversight
Strategic Objective:
To define the ultimate authority role within the Secure Living platform. The Super Admin is the root-level operator  --  empowered to configure, override, and monitor every module, while being constrained by strict security, accountability, and governance controls. This ensures that while one role can resolve emergencies and guide the platform's direction, no single individual can act without transparency and auditability.

1. Role Definition
 Super Admin = System Root Authority.
 Exists in singleton mode (max 1 - 2 accounts globally).
 Cannot be deleted without another Super Admin's explicit approval.
 May impersonate any role (executive, HR, finance, marketing, client, landlord, tenant) for oversight, troubleshooting, or compliance checks.
 Serves as final escalation endpoint across all workflows (HR, Finance, Tenancy, Marketing, Compliance).

2. Powers & Scope
🔧 System Configuration
 Create, modify, or disable any role.
 Adjust role-based scopes (branch, region, service category).
 Configure approval thresholds for Finance (KES limits), Marketing budgets, HR actions.
 Enable/disable or restrict access to modules (e.g., Marketing Action Panel, AI hooks, Escrow).
👥 User Lifecycle Oversight
 Approve or revoke any user account (staff, client, contractor, professional).
 Override delegation rules (shorten, extend, revoke).
 Force-terminate sessions or lock devices in case of security breaches.
📜 Audit & Compliance
 Unrestricted access to immutable audit logs across all modules.
 Generate instant compliance packs (AML, CFT, tenancy audits, HR investigations).
 Pull "forensic trails" for regulators.
🚨 Emergency Overrides
 Platform-wide lockdown: freeze logins system-wide during an incident.
 Finance lockdown: freeze escrow payouts during fraud events.
 Trigger Disaster Recovery Failover (geo-redundant switchover).
 Emergency HR termination with immediate data wipe.

3. Safeguards & Restrictions
 Step-Up Authentication: All Super Admin actions require re-authentication (separate password + OTP).
 Dual Control Protocols:
 Finance threshold changes (> KES 1M).
 Escrow payout rule changes.
 Audit log tampering attempts.
 DR failover initiation.
These require two Super Admins to approve.
 Immutable Logging:
Every action logged with:
 Action Type
 Justification (mandatory free-text field)
 Timestamp & IP
 Device/User-Agent
 Module or User Affected

4. Oversight Dashboards (Super Admin UI)
 System Health Monitor: Live status of core services (finance, tenancy, HR, marketing).
 Risk Heatmap: AI-driven view of arrears hotspots, fraud flags, SLA breaches.
 Escalation Queue: List of unresolved critical alerts requiring Super Admin intervention.
 Audit Trail Explorer: Advanced log filtering/search by role, branch, action type.
 Configuration Manager: History of role, scope, and threshold changes.
Security Indicators:
 🔴 Red Border --> high-risk actions (finance, DR, delegation overrides).
 ⚠️ Warning Modal --> dual-control required.
 📝 Justification prompt --> cannot proceed without typed reason.
 ⏳ Auto-timeout --> 15 mins idle = forced logout.

5. Example Workflows
A. Finance Fraud Event
 Escrow mismatch alert escalates directly.
 Super Admin triggers Finance Lockdown --> freezes all payouts.
 Requires dual Super Admin to lift freeze.
 Forensic report generated automatically.
B. Delegation Abuse
 Branch Manager assigns payout rights beyond limits.
 AI flags --> escalates to Super Admin.
 Super Admin revokes delegate role instantly.
 Audit log entry:
"Delegation scope exceeded  -  revoked by Super Admin at 14:32, 03/10/25."
C. HR Emergency Termination
 HR requests immediate contractor termination.
 Super Admin override = account frozen, sessions ended, cached data wiped.
 Auto-exit report sent to HR & Compliance.

6. Technical Implementation
Database Schema
super_admin_actions (
  action_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  super_admin_id UUID,
  action_type VARCHAR,
  target_entity UUID, -- user, role, module, etc.
  justification TEXT NOT NULL,
  requires_dual_approval BOOLEAN DEFAULT false,
  second_approver UUID, -- other Super Admin
  ip_address INET,
  user_agent TEXT,
  created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW()
);

system_config (
  config_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  config_key VARCHAR UNIQUE,
  config_value JSONB,
  requires_dual_approval BOOLEAN DEFAULT true,
  last_modified_by UUID,
  last_modified_at TIMESTAMP
);
Security Protocol (Pseudo-Code)
def super_admin_action(action, target, justification):
    if not require_step_up_auth():
        return error("Step-up authentication required")
    
    if action.risk_level == "HIGH":
        if not require_dual_super_admin_approval():
            return error("Dual approval required")
    
    log_action(
        action=action,
        target=target,
        justification=justification,
        requires_dual_approval=action.risk_level == "HIGH"
    )
    
    return execute_action(action, target)

7. Escalation Flow
Staff/Contractor --> Supervisor --> Branch Manager --> Executive --> SUPER ADMIN
 SLA Escalation Timelines:
 Supervisor = 24h
 Branch Manager = 48 - 72h
 Executive = Immediate for critical
 Super Admin = Final Authority (dual-control for systemic/high-risk events).

✅ Governance & Compliance Benefits
For Developers:
 Clear boundary of ultimate authority.
 Built-in safeguards against abuse.
 Logging schema ensures traceability.
For Executives:
 Transparency of escalations.

📢 Marketing Action Panel & Department Framework
(Role-Based Views Configurable by Super Admin)

🎯 Strategic Objective
To empower Secure Living's Marketing Department with a dedicated role-based Action Panel inside the Unified Dashboard, ensuring:
 Accountability (who approved what, when, and why).
 Performance visibility (ROI, pipeline conversion, branch-level impact).
 Integration with Finance, Sales/CRM, and Executive oversight.
Marketing focuses on inputs (campaigns, lead generation, brand visibility), while Executives focus on outputs (revenue, occupancy, company health). This section defines the tools, permissions, and workflows that make that connection seamless.

1. Role Taxonomy for Marketing
We extend the taxonomy of system roles:
user_type ENUM(
  'staff_marketing',        -- Day-to-day operators: run campaigns, push content
  'marketing_manager',      -- Oversees budgets, strategy, approvals
  'marketing_analyst'       -- Optional role: data/reporting only
)
 Staff Marketing --> Executes campaigns, drafts content, manages lead funnel.
 Marketing Manager --> Reviews/approves campaigns, controls budgets, views performance dashboards.
 Marketing Analyst --> Read-only role for reporting, analytics, and dashboards.
⚠️ All marketing roles still flow through the HR lifecycle (Hiring --> Onboarding --> Probation --> Termination).

2. Marketing Action Panel (Role-Specific Modules)
The Action Panel is modular and role-adaptive.
📌 Shared Modules (Staff + Manager + Analyst)
 Lead Funnel Management
 View all Tier 1 Applications (from the Public Application Portal).
 Filters: Source (Facebook Ads, Google, Referral), Branch, Campaign Tag.
 Assign leads to Sales or Relationship Managers.
 Campaign Tracker
 Create, draft, launch, pause, or close campaigns.
 Real-time metrics: Impressions, CTR, Conversions, CPA, ROI.
 Geo-mapped campaign reach (heatmap).
 Auto-sync with CRM pipeline (Lead --> Verified Client --> Paying Client).
 Content Hub
 Access to pre-approved materials (flyers, banners, video assets).
 Expiry rules: Content auto-expires (e.g., promos valid for 30 days).
 Drafts require Manager approval before going live.

📌 Manager-Only Modules
 Budget & Spend Controls
 Submit campaign budgets.
 Auto-approval <= KES 100,000 (configurable).
 Escrow-style workflow: >KES 100,000 --> Finance approval --> Executive approval.
 Budget Utilization Meter (spend vs approved budget).
 Performance Dashboards
 Compare ROI per campaign.
 View funnel efficiency (Leads --> Verified --> Active Clients).
 Exportable reports for Finance/Executives.
 Approval Workflow Visualizer
 See exactly where a campaign or budget request sits in the approval pipeline (e.g., "Pending Finance Review").

📌 Analyst Modules (Optional)
 Read-Only Reports
 Campaign ROI analysis.
 Branch-level lead conversion analysis.
 Compliance checks on unapproved content use.

3. Super Admin Configuration
Super Admin defines Marketing Panel settings:
 Which modules are visible to which role.
 Scope of data (branch-only vs global).
 Budget approval thresholds (different per manager).
 Allowed campaign types: digital ads, physical events, referrals, partnerships.
Config Schema
marketing_panel_config (
  panel_id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
  user_type ENUM('staff_marketing','marketing_manager','marketing_analyst'),
  module_name VARCHAR,
  enabled BOOLEAN DEFAULT true,
  approval_threshold NUMERIC,
  visible_scope ENUM('branch','global'),
  created_at TIMESTAMP,
  modified_by UUID
)

4. Accountability & Security Layers
 Immutable Audit Logs
Every campaign, content update, and budget request permanently logged.
 Dual Approval Workflows
Budget >KES 100,000 requires Finance + Executive.
 KPI Integration into HR Performance
 Staff --> Campaign execution scorecards.
 Managers --> ROI accountability.
 Risk Scoring System
Marketing roles scored for:
 Unauthorized campaigns.
 Overspend vs approved budget.
 Non-compliance with brand guidelines.

5. Example Workflows
A. Campaign Creation (Staff --> Manager)
 Staff drafts "Nairobi Tenant Drive."
 Manager reviews & approves.
 Staff executes.
 Results (CTR, Leads, CPA) logged in Campaign Tracker.
B. Budget Request (Manager --> Finance/Executive)
 Manager requests KES 250,000 ad spend.
 Finance approves --> Executive signs off.
 Funds allocated, spend tracked against campaign ROI.
C. Super Admin Change
 Super Admin disables "Content Hub" for Staff Marketing roles.
 Enables "Lead Funnel" for Managers only.
 Change applies instantly without developer involvement.

6. Position Relative to Executives
 Executives --> See Revenue, Occupancy, Global Business Health.
 Marketing --> See Lead Generation, Campaign ROI, Funnel Metrics.
 Super Admin --> Controls what Marketing can see/do.
Executives focus on outcomes, Marketing focuses on inputs, but both remain connected in one unified system.

7. Technical Implementation
Enhanced DB Schema
marketing_campaigns (
  campaign_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  campaign_name VARCHAR,
  created_by UUID,
  status ENUM('draft','pending_approval','active','paused','completed'),
  budget NUMERIC,
  spend_to_date NUMERIC,
  kpis JSONB, -- {impressions: 10000, ctr: 0.05, conversions: 500}
  branch_id UUID,
  created_at TIMESTAMP,
  approved_by UUID,
  approved_at TIMESTAMP
);

budget_requests (
  request_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  requested_by UUID,
  amount NUMERIC,
  purpose TEXT,
  status ENUM('pending','finance_approved','executive_approved','rejected'),
  campaign_id UUID, -- Links to campaign
  created_at TIMESTAMP
);

content_library (
  content_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  content_type ENUM('flyer','video','social_post','email_template'),
  status ENUM('draft','approved','expired'),
  created_by UUID,
  approved_by UUID,
  branch_id UUID,
  expires_at DATE
);

UI/UX Hooks
 Approval Workflow Visualizer --> timeline view of campaign approval pipeline.
 Budget Utilization Meter --> circular progress bar with thresholds.
 Content Expiry Alerts --> red banner + auto-email notifications.
 Campaign Performance Heatmap --> map showing geographic ROI.

Appendix: Data Migration & Portfolio Onboarding Strategy
To onboard landlords with existing portfolios, Secure Living will execute a structured data migration and verification process to ensure data integrity and system trust from day one.
 Data Template & Import: Landlords or their previous agents will be provided with a standardized CSV template to populate with their existing data, including: property details, active tenant information, lease terms, and current financial balances. This data will be imported into a "PENDING VERIFICATION" state within the Secure Living system.
 Mandatory Professional Verification Audit: Before any migrated property is activated, a Secure Living Professional must conduct an on-site "Verification Inspection." This audit will:
 Confirm the physical condition of the property (creating the initial digital inspection report).
 Verify tenant occupancy and lease details.
 Reconcile any security deposits or outstanding balances.
 This step is non-negotiable and transforms legacy data into a trusted, verified digital record, establishing a clean baseline for all future operations.



   
Secure Living: Future Expansion Phases (Post-MVP Launch)
Phase 1: Scalability & Automation
Core Focus: Hardening the core platform, integrating with key Kenyan financial and regulatory infrastructure, and automating workflows to handle significant growth.
 Key Initiatives:
 Automated Verification & Risk Mitigation:
 CRB API Integration: Embed official credit checks into the tenant screening process for objective risk scoring.
 Banking API Partnership: Integrate with a Kenyan bank for automated trust account reconciliation and real-time payment statuses.
Phase 2: Growth & Expansion
Core Focus: Launching premium value-added services, expanding the service marketplace, and establishing Secure Living as a comprehensive property ecosystem.
 Key Initiatives:
 Ecosystem & Marketplace Growth:
 Service Provider Marketplace: Launch a full marketplace where outsiders/ non members landlords and tenants can directly browse, book, and pay vetted plumbers, electricians, and cleaners.
 B2B Landlord Tiers: Develop tailored plans and discounted pricing for developers and clients with large portfolios (10+ units).
 Strategic API Integrations:
 KRA iTax API Integration: Automate the calculation, withholding, and filing of rental income tax for landlords.
Phase 3: Market Leadership & Innovation
Core Focus: Leveraging aggregated data to create new products and solidifying Secure Living as an indispensable financial and property technology platform.
 Key Initiatives:
 Financial Products:
 Rent Default Insurance: Partner with insurers to offer policies protecting against tenant non-payment.
 Property-Backed Financing: Offer loans to landlords using their managed property as collateral.
 Advanced Technologies:
 VR/AR Property Tours: Integrate 3D scanning and virtual tours for remote inspections.
 Geographic Service Expansion:
 Franchise Model: Package the technology for licensing to franchisees in new regions.
 "Move-In/Move-Out" Services: Expand into utility setup and municipal registration.
 Geospatial services
        
Geospatial & Property Listing Services
A. Geospatial Services (Verification, Analytics, Oversight)
Technical Objective:
Embed geospatial tools into Secure Living's core platform to support property verification, environmental risk analysis, and portfolio visualization.
Core Functionalities:
 Property Verification & Due Diligence
 Capture GPS coordinates and property boundaries.
 Compare uploaded survey files with official land registry data.
 Detect possible encroachments and mismatches in land size or shape.
 Assign verification status: Pending, Verified, or Flagged.
 Risk Analysis & Monitoring
 Assess flood zones, soil stability, slope/terrain, and accessibility using open data sources.
 Store property risk levels as Low / Medium / High.
 Update risk assessments periodically (every 3 - 6 months).
 Professional GIS Tools
 Mobile GPS capture for professionals with accuracy optimization.
 Upload of geotagged media (photos/videos) pinned to property locations.
 Offline-first support for rural areas with syncing once online.
 Portfolio Visualization
 Interactive map view of all properties owned by a landlord.
 Risk and verification overlays (toggle flood zones, boundaries, risk scores).
 Color-coded status: Green = Verified, Yellow = Pending, Red = High Risk.
        
B. Tenant Flood Risk Alerts
Objective:
Provide tenants with accessible flood risk information to build trust and support safe housing choices.
Features:
 Risk Classification
 Every property tagged with flood risk level (Low/Medium/High).
 Based on open data and historical flood patterns.
 Tenant-Facing Alerts
 Property listings display a clear flood risk badge.
 Active tenants receive seasonal notifications (e.g., "⚠️ Heavy rains expected  -  your area has medium flood risk").
 Educational support via links to county advisories and safety tips.
 Landlord Transparency
 Landlords notified if their property is flagged as medium or high risk.
 Option to purchase a Professional GIS Verification Report for credibility.
 Verified properties receive a "Secure Verified" badge in listings.
 Monetization Strategy
 Flood risk info is always free for tenants.
 Landlords pay for advanced reports, continuous monitoring, and verification badges (recurring or one-time module).
        
C. Property Listings & Marketplace
Objective:
Enable landlords to publish properties as public listings and allow prospective tenants or buyers to discover them with map-based search and filtering.
Core Functionalities:
 Listing Management
 Landlords mark properties as Public Listings or Private Portfolio Only.
 Listings include media (photos, videos), property type, units, price, and availability.
 Standard media quota is free; premium upgrades unlock more uploads.
 Tenant/Buyer Search & Discovery
 Map-based search with filters for location, rent/sale price, property type, and amenities.
 Listings displayed as pins on an interactive map; clicking reveals details and media.


 Premium Visibility Options
 Free Tier: Standard listings with photos.
 Premium Tier (Paid): Featured placement, higher visibility, unlimited media, and "Verified Listing" badge.
4. Decision Support Insights
 Rent benchmarking against average market prices in the same area.
 Heatmap overlays for demand based on tenant inquiries.
 Historical trend charts for resale properties.
 Market Data Collection & Insights:
 The system tracks anonymized tenant/buyer searches (location, price range, property type).
 Data is aggregated to identify properties and locations with the highest demand.
 Secure Living uses these insights to:
 Offer advisory services to landlords (pricing, upgrades, resale decisions).
 Publish anonymized market trend reports as a value-add service.
 Strategically position premium listings in high-demand areas.
        
D. System Integration & Architecture
 Backend Services
 Properties store GPS coordinates, boundaries, and risk profiles.
 Verification logs keep audit trails of assessments and professional input.
 Risk overlays stored as layers linked to properties.
 Frontend / UX Integration
 Landlord Dashboard: Map view with property overlays and request-for-verification button.
 Professional App: GPS capture, offline sync, media upload.
 Tenant Action Panel: Flood risk badges, seasonal alerts, map search.
  Clarify access layers:
 Tenant: search + filters + overlays.
 Landlord: compliance, nearby amenities for marketing.
 Staff: inspection verification, compliance confirmation
 
 Scalability
 Start with simple GPS point mapping and flood risk overlays.
 Later expand to other risks (fire, landslides, crime hotspots).
 Add advanced portfolio analytics as adoption grows.
        
E. Revenue Opportunities
 Recurring Premium Modules
 Continuous GIS monitoring with quarterly updates.
 Automated seasonal risk alerts.
 One-Time Premium Services
 GIS Verification Reports.
 Boundary and encroachment validation.
 Proxy support for registry or compliance disputes.
 Marketplace Monetization
 Premium listing upgrades (featured, verified, media expansion).
 Cross-sell verification services as upsells before publishing listings.
        
F. Technical Dependencies & Implementation Notes
        Geospatial Stack
 Base Maps: OpenStreetMap (default, free) with optional Mapbox GL JS (free tier).
 Data Processing: Turf.js for client-side calculations; PostGIS for backend storage/queries.
 File Formats: GeoJSON as standard; KML as fallback.
 Mobile: GPS averaging for accuracy + offline storage/sync for rural areas.
        Implementation Sequence
 Phase 1: Basic property mapping + flood risk badges.
 Phase 2: Boundary uploads + verification workflows.
 Phase 3: Portfolio analytics + premium features.
 Phase 4: Advanced risk layers + predictive analytics.
        Kenyan Data Sources
 Flood Data: NASA FIRMS + Kenya Meteorological Department.
 Land Registry: County-level APIs/registries (varied data quality).
 Base Maps: OpenStreetMap Kenya + local satellite imagery.
        
G. Suggested Additions for Robustness
Data Quality Handling
Kenyan geospatial data quality varies by county. The system should implement confidence scoring for verification results:
 High confidence: Urban areas with accurate registry data.
 Medium confidence: Rural areas validated mainly via satellite imagery.
 Low confidence: Conflicting or missing data --> flag for manual review.
        Scalability Considerations
 Support gradual enhancement from points --> polygons --> 3D models.
 Implement caching for external data APIs to reduce costs.
 Enable batch processing for periodic risk reassessments (e.g., quarterly).

















Section 14  --  Real Estate Data & Intelligence Framework 
Purpose (overview)
Secure Living will be a data-first property platform: every operational action must produce structured, auditable data that powers transparency, predictive intelligence, operational efficiency, and monetizable products. This Section prescribes what to collect, how and where it is collected (role/UI), why it matters, and how it must be validated, stored, governed, and converted into insights and products.

14.1 Core Data Capture Principles (the rules that must be enforced everywhere)
 Role-Based Entry
 Only authorized system users enter data:
 Supervisors / Property Managers  --  inspections, meter readings, invoice verification, proxy verification.
 Leasing Officers  --  listings, tours, onboarding, renewals, termination reasons.
 Professionals (field app)  --  work orders, components, brand, evidence.
 Tenants  --  complaints, feedback, payments, referrals.
 Admins  --  taxonomy management, verification overrides.
 Caretakers do not use the platform; caretakers report readings/receipts to Supervisors who enter and attach proof.
 Structured Inputs with Controlled Flexibility
 All critical taxonomies use dropdowns/checklists (inspections, repairs, components, termination reasons, complaint categories, counties, brands).
 Every dropdown must include "Other (Please specify)". Selecting "Other" requires the user to provide a short free-text rationale. Such entries are automatically queued for Admin review and either: (a) accepted into the taxonomy, (b) reclassified, or (c) archived as a one-off.
 Event-Sourcing & Immutable Audit Trails
 Every important action is an event (who, what, when, where, payload).
 Audit trail includes user id, role, property id, event id, timestamp, payload, verification status, and verifier id. Changes do not overwrite history  --  they append new events and record previous state.
 Transparency & Access
 Tenants and landlords see structured records per their consent and role: inspections, invoices, complaints, verification reports, lease status. Anonymized benchmarking data is available conditionally (see benchmarking rules).
 Benchmarking & Anonymization
 Secure Living may generate and sell aggregate, anonymized benchmarks (e.g., average water consumption per sq.m. in Kilimani). No single property, landlord, or tenant may be identified.
 Inclusion in anonymized datasets requires opt-in at onboarding or later explicit consent; clients may opt-out.
 Privacy & Legal Compliance
 All practices comply with the Kenya Data Protection Act (consent capture, PII minimization, retention, right-to-be-forgotten).
 Data sharing externally requires anonymization or explicit client consent.
 Trust/escrow handling must comply with Central Bank of Kenya rules and be auditable.

14.2 Data Categories & Collection Workflows (what to capture, who captures it, why it matters)
This section lists the categories and exact workflows for capture. Every item below must result in structured fields in the platform (dropdowns, numeric fields, dates, geotags, attachments).
Property Performance Metrics
 Ownership Intelligence
Segment landlords by profile (diaspora, single-unit owner, estate developer, corporate portfolio) to tailor services and advisory insights
2. Utility Billing & Benchmarking (water, electricity, gas)
 Who captures: Caretaker reads meters --> reports to Supervisor. Supervisor is the system user who inputs:
 initial_meter_reading, final_meter_reading
 billing_period_start, billing_period_end
 price_per_unit, fixed_meter_charge (meter fee if landlord charges it)
 other_charges (separate invoicing for garbage/service fees)
 meter_photo_url (optional), uploader_id, verification_status, verified_by, verified_at
 System calculation: units_used = final  -  initial; consumption_cost = units_used x price_per_unit; total_bill = consumption_cost + fixed_meter_charge.
 Validation / QA: final >= initial; numeric validation; anomaly detection vs benchmarks.
 Why: Transparent billing for tenants; leak/tamper detection; neighborhood benchmarking; inputs for advisory services.
3. Tenant Turnover by Property Age & Condition
 Capture: lease_end_reason (dropdown), property_condition_at_move_out (Good/Fair/Poor), move_out_inspection_photo.
 Why: Links condition & age to churn; informs refurbishment ROI.
4. Complaint Frequency & Resolution
 Tenant logs complaint via app: complaint_category (Noise/Water/Electricity/Security/Other), urgency, description (free-text optional), time_logged.
 Supervisor logs resolution outcome: resolved/pending/escalated, resolution_notes, closure_time, tenant_rating.
 Why: SLA compliance, livability metrics, service quality analytics.
B. Tenant Lifecycle & Preference Data
1. Listing & Tour Data
 Listings capture: listing_id, property_id, listing_type (public/private), unit_type, bedrooms, bathrooms, furnished_flag, rent, media_urls, verified_flag, gps_coordinates.
 Preference Insights: Track tenant preferences not just by property type and rent level, but also by finishes and house design features (e.g., open kitchens, tiled floors, solar installations).
 Tour booking flow: prospect_id, scheduled_time, agent_id, tour_outcome (attended/no-show), tour_feedback (structured tags + NPS), would_refer (Yes/No/Maybe).
 If the prospect declines: decline_reason (Too Expensive, Location, Condition, Competing Listing, Timing, Other) + optional comment.
 Why: Conversion funnel analytics, pricing/feature adjustment, agent performance metrics.
2. Screening & Onboarding
 Capture CRB_result, identity_verified_flag, payment_method_selected (M-Pesa, Bank, Card), deposit_received (yes/no).
 Why: Tenant risk scoring; payment channel optimization.
3. Move-in / Move-out Seasonality
 Lease start/end auto-logged for seasonal heatmaps and demand forecasting.
4. Renewal Negotiation & Retention
 Capture renewal_offer_details, negotiation_tactic (Discount/Upgrade/Other), outcome, retention_reason.
 Why: Build empirically validated retention playbooks.
5. Referral / NPS
 Capture NPS_score, would_recommend boolean, referral_consent, referral_contact optional.
 Why: Identify promoters for referral campaigns.
C. Repairs, Maintenance, & Construction Oversight
1. Work Orders (full lifecycle)
 Creation: reported_by, issue_category, description, reported_time, evidence_photos.
 Assignment: assigned_to (professional_id), accept_time, travel_estimate.
 Completion: completion_time, parts_used (component, brand, qty), parts_cost, labor_cost, resolution_type (Repair/Replace/Upgrade/TemporaryFix), warranty_offered, evidences (photos/videos), tenant_rating.
 Warranty & Material Use Tracking: Cross-reference service provider jobs with the brands of materials used --> identify providers who consistently use durable vs. unreliable products.
 Verification: supervisor_verification_flag, verified_by, verified_at.
 Why: Predictive maintenance, supplier benchmarking, SLA metrics.
2. Construction Oversight
 Capture: contract_id, milestones (description, value, required_evidence), milestone_evidence (geotagged photos/videos), inspection_reports, snags_list, final_signoff.
 Why: Enable diaspora construction oversight, trigger milestone-based escrow releases.
3. Warranty & Brand Durability
 Capture installation_date, brand, model, failure_logs, warranty_expiry.
 Why: Warranty claims automation & supplier negotiation.
 Brand & Material Intelligence
 Capture the specific brand and model of items installed or replaced (e.g., showerheads, sockets, tiles, locks).
 Track frequency of failure/replacement per brand --> create durability rankings.
 Link brand performance with supplier/vendor source.
 Benchmark cost vs. lifespan to score value-for-money.
 Use insights for:
 Construction oversight (recommending or avoiding certain brands).
 Procurement partnerships (bulk deals with durable brands).
 Long-term expansion into a Secure Living - approved hardware supply chain.
  
D. Proxy Services, Consignment & Due Diligence (Diaspora-focused services)
1. Proxy Services
 Capture request_id, client_id, proxy_assigned, purpose (attend meeting/pick document), request_date, proof_of_execution (signed document photo, receipt), funds_held, payment_release_status.
 Workflow: client request --> assign proxy --> proxy completes --> uploads proof --> supervisor verifies --> funds released per rule.
 Legal: require uploaded POA for actions requiring representation.
2. Consignment Purchases
 Capture consignment_id, asset_type (land/property/furniture), purchase_price, supplier_details, receipts, title_transfer_docs, client_approval_flags, funds_in_trust, disbursements.
 All consignment purchases should follow escrow rules and generate complete audit trail.
3. Due Diligence
 Capture survey_files, title_search_result, encumbrance_flags, county_permits, surveyor_comments, GIS_boundary_match.
 Why: Protect diaspora clients and document risk.
E. Financial Systems & Trust / Milestone Payments
1. Trust Accounts & Escrow/Milestone Logic
 Capture transaction events: deposit_to_trust, funds_allocated_to_project, evidence_required_for_release, release_event, approver_id.
 Release rules: fund release only when required_evidence uploaded and verified, or when predefined milestone event occurs and verified.
 Why: Prevents unilateral disbursements; builds diaspora trust.
2. Payment & Reconciliation
 Capture payment_method, transaction_ref, settlement_status, reconciliation_match_flag, reconciled_by.
 Integrations: M-Pesa as primary local rail; bank & card rails for diaspora.
F. Geospatial & Property Listing Services 
1. Property Geotagging & Boundaries
 Capture GPS coordinate(s) and boundary polygon (survey file), survey_upload, geotagged photos/videos.
 Use: map-based search, verification vs registry, detect encroachments.
2. Location Context & Catchment Analytics
 Capture nearby POIs (schools, hospitals, malls, transport nodes), travel_times (drive/walk), network connectivity score (mobile signal captured at inspection).
 Use: compute location premium, inform pricing/marketing.
3. Risk Overlays & Flood Alerts
 Capture flood_risk_level, slope/terrain_notes, access_score, soil_stability. Tie to seasonal alerts for tenants.
4. Vacancy Heatmaps & Tenant Migration
 Aggregate geospatial vacancy rates and tenant origin/destination data (previous neighborhood captured in onboarding).
 Use: detect migration trends and supply imbalances.

Automated Listing & Geo-Location Intelligence

15.1 Purpose
This module transforms Secure Living into an automated vacancy, listing, and geospatial intelligence engine. The goal is to:
 Eliminate vacancy lag by auto-detecting upcoming unit availability.
 Create seamless workflows from tenant move-out --> listing --> prospect notification --> new lease.
 Give tenants transparent, map-based search with rich overlays (schools, transport, utilities, risk).
 Capture behavioral and demand signals (decline reasons, saved searches, Notify Me subscriptions) to power analytics and monetizable data products.
 Differentiate Secure Living in Kenya's market by fusing property lifecycle automation + geospatial intelligence + demand forecasting.

15.2 Vacating Notice & Vacancy Lifecycle
Tenant Vacating Notice (Tenant App)
 Form captures:
 unit_id (auto-linked to current lease)
 planned_move_out_date (system enforces 30-day or configured notice period)
 reason_for_leaving (dropdown: rent too high, relocation, poor condition, job transfer, other)
 refund_channel (bank account, M-Pesa, wallet)
 forwarding_address (optional, for deposit/communication)
System Actions
 Lease flagged "Ending on [date]."
 Unit status --> "Upcoming Vacancy."
 Supervisor receives task: inspection scheduled automatically before vacate date.
 Landlord alerted: "Unit B2 will be vacated on March 15."
 Deposit refund workflow triggered and linked to inspection results. Deposit refunds are only initiated once inspection status = "passed" and any approved deductions are confirmed by landlord. Ensures fairness and dispute prevention.
    Refund Breakdown: Tenants should see a clear refund summary after inspection:
   :: Deposit Paid: KES [amount]
   :: Deducted (repairs/arrears): KES [amount]
   :: Refundable Balance: KES [amount]
   Refund Timeline: Refund to be released within 14 business days.
   
 Draft listing auto-created with status: "Available from [vacate date]".
Landlord Dashboard
 Dashboard shows:
 "Upcoming vacancies" section with countdown timers.
 Draft listings pending supervisor verification.
 Inspection checklist auto-linked to unit.
 Landlord can pre-approve marketing spend (premium listing, highlight badge, verified stamp).
Forced Vacancy Trigger (Supervisor Override)
 If tenant abandons unit without notice:
 Supervisor marks "Vacant After Inspection."
 Listing status = "Available Now."
 Deposit refund status = "On Hold (No Notice)."
Automated Vacancy Detection:
If no rent is received for 30 consecutive days and no vacating notice is submitted, 
the system should automatically flag the unit as "Suspected Vacancy" and prompt 
a supervisor inspection. This prevents false "Upcoming Vacancy" statuses and 
ensures abandoned units are detected quickly.
Why Important:
 Landlord: no downtime, transparent process, deposit linked to evidence.
 Tenant: structured exit, predictable deposit refund.
 System: auto-creates listing pipeline, no manual delays.

15.3 Auto-Listing Engine
Data Auto-Populated
 From property master: property_id, unit_type, bedrooms, bathrooms, rent, amenities, GPS coordinates, media.
 From last inspection: condition score, amenity verification, updated photos if < 12 months old.
 From billing system: average utility cost ranges for transparency.
Status Logic
 "Available from [vacate date]" --> immediately after tenant notice submitted.
 "Available Now" --> only after inspection and supervisor confirmation.
Media & Freshness Check
 If last photo set > 12 months old, Supervisor prompted to upload fresh images before listing goes public.
Premium Listing Enhancements
 Verified Badge = only for properties with full compliance documents.
 Highlight Badge = available if landlord subscribes to marketing add-on.
 Priority Placement = system promotes based on landlord subscription tier.
Audit Trails
 Every auto-listing carries: created_by_system_flag, source_event_id (vacating_notice, supervisor_override, landlord_manual).

15.4 Prospect Notifications & Demand Capture
Notification Subscriptions
 Unit-specific: Prospect clicks "Notify Me" --> linked to that exact unit.
 Saved Search Profile: Prospect saves criteria (location, rent, unit size, amenities). Stored as search_profile_id.
Notification Triggers
 Vacating notice submitted --> matching saved profiles notified.
 New property listing added.
 Supervisor confirms vacancy after inspection --> status switches from "Upcoming" to "Available Now."
Notification Channels
 In-app push notifications.
 Email alerts (for diaspora).
 SMS/WhatsApp (localized to Kenya, optional opt-in).
Landlord Transparency
 Landlord dashboard shows:
 "23 tenants subscribed to 2BR in Kilimani (KES 25k - 30k)."
 "5 prospects waiting for your unit B2."
 Helps set rent and speed up leasing.
Demand Signals Captured
 search_profile_id, timestamp, criteria (rent, location, size, amenities).
 notification_sent_flag, notification_opened_flag, response (viewing booked / ignored).

15.5 Geo-Location Finder (Tenant Search Experience)
Search Inputs
 Location: estate, county, "near me" (GPS), or manual map pin.
 Filters: rent range, unit type, bedrooms, furnished/unfurnished, amenities (balcony, borehole, parking, Wi-Fi, security, generator, water reliability).
 Radius filter: e.g., "Within 5 km of Nairobi CBD."
Results Display
 Dual interface:
 Map pins --> click to view property details.
 List view --> synchronized with map.
 Status distinction: "Available Now" (green) vs. "Available from [date]" (amber).
 Listings enriched with rent benchmarks: "KES 28k  -  5% above Kilimani average."
Tenant Journey Example
 Prospect searches for 1BR Kilimani <= 30k.
 Sees 3 units available now, 2 units upcoming next month.
 Subscribes to upcoming unit --> receives notification when inspection completes.
Map Display Modes:
The property map should prioritize local tenant usability. 
Default = Simple View, showing only available units, rent, and availability dates in a clean map format. 
Advanced View = optional toggle for users who want to see extra overlays such as nearby schools, hospitals, transport hubs, and environmental risk zones. 

Rationale: Most local tenants value quick, simple navigation to find affordable housing without complexity, 
while advanced layers support users who require more detailed decision-making. 
This keeps the interface friendly for everyday renters but powerful enough for informed comparisons. 
Clarification Note
The geo-location finder is primarily designed for local tenants in Kenya, helping them search for and discover rental units based on their current or preferred location.
For diaspora landlords and investor clients, the same geospatial framework powers market intelligence dashboards instead of property search. This includes overlays such as demand vs. supply heatmaps, risk zones, and proximity to amenities.
👉 Important: This tool does not imply property sales. Secure Living remains focused on rental management. Any property sales functionality, if ever introduced, would be part of a later growth phase.

Local Tenants (Primary Use):
The geo-location finder is primarily designed for local tenants seeking rental options. It enables them to search properties by location, filter by rent, amenities, and transport access, and visualize proximity to essential services such as schools, hospitals, and markets.
Diaspora Clients (Research + Proxy Engagement):
For diaspora clients, this feature serves mainly as a research and decision-support tool -- helping them understand neighborhoods, rental values, and demand hotspots. Importantly, it also acts as a call-to-action: diaspora clients are encouraged to hire Secure Living as their eyes, ears, and hands on the ground. Through proxy and oversight services, they can request:
 Verification of neighborhoods or specific listings.
 Due diligence checks on rental or investment opportunities.
 Representation for compliance, onboarding, and ongoing oversight.



15.6 Infrastructure & Amenities Overlays
Overlay Layers on Map
 Education: schools, universities.
 Health: hospitals, clinics.
 Transport: matatu stages, bus stops, train stations.
 Commercial: supermarkets, malls, open markets.
 Risk: flood zones, landslide risk, high-crime areas (via county API feeds).
 Utilities: water reliability index, power stability index (collected from tenant complaints + supervisor logs).
 Connectivity: mobile network coverage test (captured at inspection by Supervisor app ping).
Data Sources
 OpenStreetMap (base).
 Kenya Open Data Action Panel.
 NASA/Copernicus datasets (flood, terrain).
 County APIs (safety, compliance).
 Internal system data (complaint heatmaps --> e.g., frequent water outages).
Tenant View Example
Tenant clicks a property --> overlay shows:
 500m to nearest school.
 Flood risk = medium.
 Water reliability score = 4/5.

Multi-Role Mapping Tool (Extension of Geo-Location Finder)
The mapping tool is not only tenant-facing but also a multi-role feature with role-based permissions.
1. Local Tenants
 Search for rentals near current or desired location.
 Apply filters: price, unit type, amenities (parking, balcony, Wi-Fi, security).
 View Available Now vs. Available Soon statuses.
 Save searches and subscribe to alerts.
2. Diaspora Clients
 Research properties remotely with overlays: schools, hospitals, transport hubs, shopping centers, and risk zones.
 Compare neighborhoods and market trends before committing to investment or relocation.
 Access due diligence insights linked to property verification workflows.
3. Internal Staff / Professionals
 Conduct inspections with geotagged media uploads.
 Verify boundaries, confirm property location accuracy, and flag encroachments.
 Overlay infrastructure and environmental risks to support landlord advisory.
 Log complaints/issues to exact coordinates for analysis.
 Collect geospatial data that feeds directly into compliance and risk dashboards.

🔄 Integration with Data Intelligence
All map activities must feed into the analytics engine:
 Tenant Search Heatmaps --> show demand clusters by area, rent, and amenities.
 Inspection Logs --> generate maintenance/reliability heatmaps (e.g., water outages, frequent electrical faults).
 Vacancy Mapping --> identify areas with long downtime vs. high turnover.
 Risk Zoning --> update landlord and compliance dashboards with flood/encroachment alerts.
This ensures geospatial data is not isolated, but continuously enriches the intelligence products.

🎨 Developer UI/UX Guidelines
To ensure the mapping tool is friendly and intuitive for all roles:
 Role-Based Layers  -  System should auto-hide irrelevant overlays (e.g., compliance risks not shown to tenants).
 Interactive Map Pins  -  Clicking on a property should open a clean card with: rent, photos/videos, unit status, and available dates.
 Layer Toggles  -  Easy ON/OFF switches for overlays like schools, hospitals, transport.
 Search Simplicity  -  Tenants and diaspora clients should see a single search bar ("Where do you want to live / research?") with filters sliding out from the side.
 Offline Mode for Staff  -  Supervisors in low-connectivity areas should capture GPS points and upload when online.
 Mobile First  -  Ensure the interface is touch-friendly, with large tap areas for pins and buttons.
 Color Coding  -  Use intuitive color markers:
 Green = Available Now
 Amber = Upcoming Vacancy
 Red = Risk Alert


 Data Capture & Intelligence Outputs
Captured Data Points
 Tenant vacating reasons (arrears, relocation, poor condition).
 Tour decline reasons (Too Expensive, Wrong Location, Poor Condition, Other).
 Notify Me subscriptions (area + rent demand signals).
 Vacancy lifecycle timestamps: notice --> inspection --> listing --> occupancy.
 Saved search heatmaps by rent + amenity preferences.
Intelligence Generated
 Vacancy Heatmaps: visualize areas with long downtime vs fast turnover.
 Demand vs. Supply Gap Analysis: "Kilimani has 120 prospects for 2BR, only 45 units listed."
 Rent Adjustment Recommendations: "Landlords in Kileleshwa reduce vacancy by lowering rent 7%."
 Tenant Migration Trends: Onboarding captures "previous neighborhood." --> trend mapping (e.g., migration from Eastlands --> Upper Hill).
 Managerial Decision Tracking: When landlords/managers ignore rent adjustment advice, system logs impact --> "Your decision kept Unit B2 vacant 14 extra days."
Visit & Decline Feedback
Tenant visits and tour outcomes are central to closing the loop between marketing, listings, and leasing. Secure Living must capture this systematically.
A. Visit Tracking
 Each scheduled tour logged with prospect_id, agent_id, scheduled_time.
 Outcome recorded: Attended / No-show.
 Feedback form: Quick tags (Liked Unit, Good Price, Poor Condition, Location Issue).
B. Decline Reasons
 If tenant declines: dropdown with options (Too Expensive, Location, Condition, Competing Listing, Timing, Other).
 Optional free-text allowed only when "Other" selected.
 All decline events stored with timestamp + property_id.
C. Referral/NPS Prompt
 At tour completion or lease decline: "Would you recommend this property to a friend?" Yes/No/Maybe + 1 - 5 rating.
 Feeds into Net Promoter Score (NPS) at property/portfolio level.
D. Notification Handling
 If unit is filled after a tenant has saved/subscribed, system auto-sends:
"Unit B2 in Kilimani has been taken. Would you like to be notified when a similar unit becomes available?"
E. Analytics Use
 Identifies consistent decline reasons --> informs landlord pricing/renovation.
 Tracks agent performance based on conversion ratio.
 Builds demand heatmaps for future development and investor advisory.


G.8 Data Validation & Governance
G.8 Listing Photos & Video Policy (Expanded Final Draft)
Purpose
Photos and videos are a critical tenant-facing element of listings. In Kenya, tenants always conduct physical visits before signing leases, but media assets shape first impressions, generate interest, and set expectations. Therefore, listings must always display photos (and optional short videos) that accurately reflect the property's features. This section defines who uploads media, when updates occur, quality standards, lifecycle rules, and how assets interact with listing status.

G.8.1 Roles & Responsibilities
 Supervisors: Primary system users for uploading, updating, and removing media. They capture new photos at inspections and ensure compliance with standards.
 Caretakers: Forward photos to Supervisors if needed but never interact with the system directly.
 Landlords: View-only access. Can request updates but cannot directly upload.
 System/Platform: Enforces timestamps, archives old photos, and controls media visibility based on listing status.

G.8.2 Initial Listing Photos
 When a unit is flagged "Upcoming Vacancy" (tenant submitted vacating notice), the system automatically generates a draft listing.
 Old/existing photos are immediately attached to the draft listing, ensuring the property is visible to prospective tenants without delays.
 This allows early marketing before inspection or cleaning is completed, minimizing vacancy downtime.

G.8.3 Media Update Workflow
 Trigger Point: At tenant move-out inspection, Supervisor captures fresh photos and (optionally) a short video walkthrough.
 Upload & Replace: New media is uploaded into the system and replaces the old set.
 Archiving: Old media is not deleted  --  it is archived internally, with a timestamp, for compliance and dispute resolution.
 Verification Step: The system may prompt "Media last updated [date]" so both landlords and tenants see freshness.

G.8.4 Photo Standards
 Mandatory Shots (each must be included):
 Living room
 Kitchen
 Bathroom(s)
 Bedroom(s)
 Balcony/parking/exterior view
 Optional Shots (if applicable):
 Estate-level amenities (pool, gym, borehole, generator, solar, security).
 Quality Rules:
 Photos must be clear, horizontal orientation preferred.
 No heavy edits, watermarks, or misleading angles.
 Minimum resolution threshold enforced (e.g., 720p).

G.8.5 Video Links 
 Supervisors may attach a YouTube-hosted walkthrough video (2 - 3 minutes).
 The link is embedded into the listing and displayed alongside photos.
 Optional, not mandatory. If used, must show continuous footage of main rooms to avoid misleading edits.

G.8.6 Landlord & Tenant Transparency
 Landlords:
 Can see the timestamp of last photo update.
 Receive notifications when new media is uploaded for their property.
 Tenants:
 See both availability status ("Available Now" or "Available from [date]") and media timestamp.
 Optional video link is displayed with a clear label: "Walkthrough Video."

G.8.7 Automatic Removal & Filled Listings
 Once a lease is signed:
 Listing is automatically marked "Filled" or removed from active search results.
 Tenants who had "saved" or subscribed to the listing receive an automatic message:
 "Unit B2 in Kilimani has been taken. Would you like to be notified when a similar unit becomes available?"
 Media (photos & video) is archived internally for historical reference but hidden from public view.
For tenant visit outcomes and decline feedback that link directly to listing performance, see Section the next section how they link you

Integration Between Section 15 (Automated Listing & Geo-Location Finder) and G.8 (Photos & Video Policy)
 Trigger Point
 Tenant submits vacating notice --> system flags unit as "Upcoming Vacancy."
 G.8.2: At that same moment, the system attaches existing photos (and video if present) to the draft listing.
 Draft Listing Creation
 Draft listing auto-generated with rent, amenities, availability date.
 G.8.2 ensures media is attached immediately, avoiding delays in marketing.
 Inspection & Media Update
 After tenant vacates, Supervisor conducts inspection.
 G.8.3: Supervisor captures and uploads new photos/video, replacing old ones while archiving previous media.
 Tenant-Facing Display
 Prospects search via geo-location/map and filters.
 G.8.4 & G.8.5: They see required photos, optional estate shots, and possibly a YouTube walkthrough link.
 Status & Transparency
 Listings show "Available Now" or "Available from [date]."
 G.8.6: Media timestamp + landlord notifications + optional walkthrough visible.
 Filling the Unit
 Once lease is signed, system marks listing as filled.
 G.8.7: Photos/videos archived internally but removed from public view. Prospects who saved listing receive a "Unit Taken" notification with option to subscribe for similar units.


G.8.8 Developer Notes (System Rules)
 Every listing must always display at least 1 photo.
 Photos default to last verified upload, but system allows "use old photos until new ones available."
 All uploads tagged with: uploader_id, role, property_id, timestamp, listing_id, file_reference.
 Archived photos remain linked to listing_id but set as visibility=false for tenants.
 System auto-prompts Supervisors if media older than 12 months remains active.

G.9 Benefits & Strategic Value
 Landlords:
 Shorter vacancy periods (predictive pipeline).
 Pricing intelligence from demand heatmaps.
 Transparent competition benchmarking.
 Tenants:
 Visibility into upcoming vacancies before they hit the open market.
 Smarter search with infrastructure overlays.
 Trust via transparent inspection-based listings.
 Secure Living:
 Proprietary demand + vacancy data moat.
 Monetizable landlord dashboards, developer subscriptions, market reports.
 Differentiation from traditional agents & listing sites.

G.10 Example Events (for Developer Mapping)
 vacating_notice_submitted
 lease_flagged_upcoming_end
 draft_listing_created
 supervisor_inspection_completed
 listing_status_updated (Available from / Available Now)
 notify_me_subscribed
 notify_me_triggered
 search_profile_saved
 tour_booked
 tour_declined_with_reason
 overlay_data_updated (flood/safety/POI)
 demand_gap_report_generated
G.11 Technical & UX Enhancements (Polish Items)
This subsection introduces small but high-impact refinements that improve transparency for tenants, give landlords actionable intelligence, and make the search/listing journey smoother. These are lightweight to implement, yet significantly boost trust and user satisfaction.

A. Tenant Search Profile Refinements
 Maximum Commute Time: Allow tenants to indicate a preferred maximum travel time (in minutes) to their workplace, school, or other destination.
 Use: Even without advanced API subscriptions, this can be handled through simple radius-based filtering (e.g., "within 5km of Nairobi CBD") or approximations. Over time, richer integrations can be added.
 Benefit: Tenants feel understood, and landlords can see where demand clusters exist relative to city centers.
 Must-Have Amenities: Tenants can tag certain amenities as "non-negotiable" (e.g., backup power, water tank, parking).
 Use: Landlords learn which features drive deal-breakers, while the system can prioritize matching properties that meet the full checklist.
 Benefit: Improves search precision and reduces wasted viewings.

B. Rent Benchmarking
 Display: Each listing should show how its rent compares with the average for similar units in the same neighborhood. Example: "KES 30,000  --  8% above Kilimani average of KES 27,800."
 How it works: Secure Living computes rolling averages from existing listings and past leases within the same GPS cluster and unit type.
 Why it matters:
 Tenants gain confidence they're paying a fair market rate.
 Landlords can price competitively, reducing vacancy periods.
 Secure Living builds credibility as a trusted market intelligence source.

C. Water Reliability Score
 Display: A simple score (1 - 5 stars or 0 - 100 index) shown on property pages and landlord dashboards.
 Inputs:
 Frequency of water-related complaints from tenants.
 Presence of backup infrastructure (borehole, storage tanks).
 Supervisor verification notes.
 Benefit:
 Tenants make better decisions, avoiding "hidden" water shortage issues.
 Landlords are incentivized to invest in reliable infrastructure, boosting retention.
 Note: This is computed from internal logs only  --  no external subscriptions needed.

D. Fast-Fill Potential Indicator
 Definition: A simple "High / Medium / Low" tag predicting how quickly a unit is likely to be rented.
 Inputs:
 Number of active "Notify Me" subscriptions that match the unit's profile.
 Past conversion rates for similar units in the same area.
 Pricing relative to benchmark (e.g., priced below average = faster fill).
 Display: On landlord dashboards and draft listings, e.g., "High: Expected fill < 7 days".
 Benefit:
 Landlords get early signals to adjust pricing or enable premium placement.
 Tenants gain confidence that a popular unit is in demand.

E. Implementation Notes
 No costly subscriptions needed: Commute filters can start with radius search. Environmental overlays or advanced APIs can be added later if demand grows.
 Materialize the insights from existing data: Benchmarks, reliability scores, and fast-fill indicators come directly from tenant complaints, inspections, and saved searches already captured in the system.
 Explainability: Every score or indicator should be shown with a short tooltip (e.g., "2 water complaints in last 3 months; borehole present; score = 78/100"). This transparency builds trust.
 Privacy safeguards: All insights must be anonymized and aggregated  --  no individual tenant complaint is directly shown; only the overall reliability or benchmark outcome.

G. Market & Strategic Intelligence
 Rent benchmarking, vacancy trends, demand heatmaps, infrastructure scoring, gentrification early warnings, supplier performance by region.
 Data products: internal dashboards, paid developer subscriptions, market reports (anonymized), API feeds to partners.

14.3 Data Capture Interfaces & Dropdown Integration (exact UI mapping)
Below lists every role UI and the dropdowns / fields they must use. Dropdown lists are centrally managed in Admin.
Supervisor / Property Inspector Dashboard (web + mobile)
 Use cases: inspections, move-in/out, meter logging, verification.
 Required dropdowns/fields: property_condition (Good/Fair/Poor), amenity_checklist (Balcony, Parking, Borehole, Solar, CCTV, Generator), meter_initial_reading, meter_final_reading, price_per_unit, fixed_meter_fee, meter_photo_url, verification_status (Pending/Verified/Flagged), complaint_resolution_outcome, inspection_comments (free text optional), other_selected_reason (free-text, Admin review required).
Professional (Field) App
 Use cases: accept & complete tasks, upload evidence, parts quoting.
 Required dropdowns/fields: repair_category, component_type, brand (from master list), resolution_type (Repair/Replace/Upgrade/TemporaryFix), parts_cost, labor_cost, warranty_length, evidence_photos, professional_notes.
Leasing Officer Dashboard
 Use cases: listed properties, tour scheduling, tenant onboarding, renewals, termination.
 Required dropdowns/fields: listing_type, unit_type, furnished_flag, rent, tour_slot, tour_feedback_tags, tour_outcome, decline_reason (structured list + "Other"), renewal_outcome, negotiation_tactic, deposit_status, referral_consent, NPS_score.
Tenant Mobile App
 Use cases: complaints, payments, feedback, referrals.
 Required dropdowns/fields: complaint_category, urgency (High/Med/Low), would_recommend, feedback_rating, payment_method_choice.
Admin Panel
 Master taxonomies: amenities, components, brands, counties, complaint_reasons.
 Admin workflows: review "Other" entries, update taxonomies, manage verification rules, set mandatory fields, view audit logs, manage user roles.
  * Managerial decisions --> tracked as events.
  * Metrics for operational workers vs. data workers.
  * Collaboration/innovation/learning KPIs.
  * Weighting (rollout --> growth --> optimization).
 
Other Handling Flow
 When a user selects "Other":
 Require short free-text reason.
 Automatically log an event and queue it in Admin review dashboard.
 Admin accepts & adds taxonomy item, rejects (keeps as one-off), or merges into existing category.
 Once admin actioned, all related events are annotated accordingly.

14.4 Data Storage, Event Model & Logical Data References (developer facing; no code)
Event-driven approach (conceptual): every UI action produces an event (event_type, user_id, role, property_id, timestamp, payload_summary, session_id, verification_status). Events are the canonical source of truth for history and ML features.
Suggested logical records / core entities (field lists only):
 Properties: property_id, owner_id, address, gps_coordinates, boundary_reference, verification_status, completeness_score, listing_id.
 Listings: listing_id, property_id, public_flag, price, unit_type, bedrooms, furnished_flag, media_urls, listed_at, agent_id.
 Leases: lease_id, property_id, tenant_id, start_date, end_date, rent, deposit_amount, payment_terms.
 MeterReadings/UtilityInvoices: id, property_id, period_start, period_end, initial_reading, final_reading, units_used, price_per_unit, fixed_charge, total_amount, uploaded_by, verified_by, status, raw_file_url.
 WorkOrders/Tasks: task_id, property_id, created_by, assigned_professional, category, component, brand, parts_cost, labor_cost, evidence_urls, status, tenant_rating, warranty_info.
 Events: event_id, event_type, user_id, property_id, timestamp, payload (structured), session_id, verification_status.
 Professionals: professional_id, name, license_number, license_expiry, rating, service_areas.
 ProxyRequests & Consignments: id, client_id, service_type, assigned_proxy, funds_held, proof_uploads, status, final_documents_url.
 Users: user_id, role, assigned_properties, timezone, preferred_currency, notification_preferences.
 AuditLogs: log_id, event_id, change_summary, changed_by, changed_at.
 Taxonomies: amenity_list, component_list, brand_list, complaint_categories, counties.
 GeospatialLayers: property_id, gps_point, boundary_polygon_ref, flood_risk, soil_notes, POI_list.
(Developers will normalize these into physical tables & indexes appropriate for chosen DB.)

14.5 Data Quality, Validation & Taxonomy Management (rules & flows)
Validation rules examples
 Meter readings numeric; final >= initial.
 Dates valid and start < end.
 Price and amounts numeric & within plausible bounds.
 Dropdown values must exist; "Other" triggers admin review.
 Image evidence presence when required for invoice or milestone.
Completeness & scoring
 Each property has a completeness score (0 - 100) derived from: title docs present, last inspection date, recent meter reading, listing media quality, verification badge.
 Properties below a threshold (e.g., 70) appear on supervisor priority list.
Anomaly detection & automated flags
 Consumption spikes vs neighborhood baseline.
 Duplicate title numbers across properties.
 Rapid churn (multiple move-outs in a short window).
 Automated alerts to supervisors + admin for human review.
Strategic Intelligence Applications
 For Landlords: Actionable dashboards with pricing benchmarks, material durability insights, and occupancy trends.
 For Secure Living: Proprietary data on repairs, brands, tenant demand, and landlord profiles to drive new revenue streams (hardware supply, advisory, bulk procurement).
 For the Market: Aggregated intelligence can inform construction companies, policymakers, and developers about real material performance and tenant needs
Taxonomy change management
 Admin review queue shows "Other" submissions with counts.
 Admin acceptance updates the central taxonomy and annotates historical records where relevant.

14.6 System Resilience, Sync & Conflict Resolution (deterministic rules)
Offline-first behavior
 Mobile apps (supervisor/professional) allow offline capture of inspections, meter readings, and evidence. Events are queued locally until network available.
Sync rules & conflicts
 Deterministic conflict resolution:
 Default: most recent timestamp wins for non-critical fields.
 Authority override: Supervisor edits override Tenant edits when conflict pertains to billing, verification, or inspection outcomes.
 All conflicts are logged and flagged for admin review; both versions preserved in audit log.
 No destructive deletes: deletions recorded as events with reason and approver.
Backup & RTO/RPO targets
 Critical transactional data (payments, leases) --> near-real-time replication. Target RTO <= 1 hour, RPO <= 15 minutes.
 Operational data (inspections/tasks) --> hourly snapshots, daily full backups.
 Archival logs preserved long-term per retention policy.
 Regular recovery drills with documented results and corrective actions.

14.7 API, Integrations & Third-party Management
Key external integrations
 Payments: M-Pesa (primary local), bank rails, international remittance rails for diaspora.
 Registries: Lands Registry / surveyor feeds, CRB screening, county compliance feeds.
 GIS / Maps: mapping provider and local geodata for flood & terrain overlays.
 Weather & Alerts: for seasonal maintenance triggers.
API management & resilience
 Rate limiting & priority routing (payments high priority).
 Circuit breakers & retry strategies with exponential backoff.
 Local cache for frequently used taxonomies and reference data.
 Fallback sources for critical feeds (secondary mapping, weather API).

Staff Enablement, Adoption & Behavioral Incentives
Training
 Interactive onboarding modules embedded into dashboards (must pass test before unlock).
 Role-specific curricula for Supervisors, Leasing Officers, Professionals, Admins.
Real-time coaching
 Contextual micro-prompts during data entry (e.g., "Photo evidence reduces disputes by X%  --  please attach.").
Quick wins & immediate feedback
 "Your impact this week" widget: shows real cases where staff entries prevented loss or saved time/money.
Gamification
 Points for 100% complete entries, streak badges, leaderboards by role.
 Monthly recognition & rewards to keep data quality high.
Operational rules
 Mandatory fields enforced  --  no closure of inspections, work orders, or invoices until required dropdowns filled and, where needed, supervisor verification given.

Data Migration & Onboarding of Existing Portfolios
Migration approach
 Staged batches by region/property cluster to limit blast radius.
 Pre-migration validation rules and sampling.
 Rollback capability per batch; parallel running until validation complete.
Validation checks
 Data integrity scoring per property post-migration.
 Reconciliation of balances, leases, and outstanding tasks.
Phased content to migrate (ordered by priority)
 Property master records & active leases.
 Current tenant balances & deposit data.
 Last 12 months of work orders and meter readings.
 Historical communications and disputes (as archival).

Data Intelligence, Analytics & Products (how data becomes value)
Data Sources: Intelligence is generated from daily operational data including repairs logged (with part/brand details), tenant payment behaviors, listing engagement, compliance filings, and supervisor inspection reports.
Core Analytics Use-Cases
:: Predictive maintenance: forecast failures and schedule preventive work.
:: Arrears & eviction risk: early warning signals from payment & behavior patterns. 
:: Brand & supplier benchmarking: track failure rates, durability, and cost-effectiveness of materials across brands, providers, and counties.
:: Dynamic pricing: rent guidance via seasonality, amenity premiums, and geospatial demand.
:: AVM: automated property valuation using rental comps, infrastructure signals, and geodata.
Tenant Insights
Dimensions e.g., migration trends, most requested amenities, dissatisfaction reasons. This makes it more balanced for product intelligence and future advisory
Dashboards by Audience
:: Operations: work order SLAs, technician efficiency, spare parts/material replacement alerts.
:: Leasing: conversion rates, top decline reasons, agent performance.
:: Finance: trust balances, disbursement status, revenue leakage.
:: Executive: CLTV, market penetration, strategic expansion signals.
Monetizable Products
:: Landlord premium dashboards (subscription).
:: Developer subscription (real-time demand, design preferences, heatmaps).
:: Market reports (e.g., Kenya Rental Index).
:: API access to anonymized market feeds for partners.
Privacy & Anonymization:
All analytics provided in dashboards or market reports must be anonymized and aggregated. 
No individual landlord, tenant, or property can be identified in shared insights. 
Developer Note: enforce anonymization through hashed IDs before analysis.

Expanded Framework for Developer Implementation
Objective
Every activity on the Secure Living platform should generate structured data points that are reusable across different perspectives. Data is captured passively during normal operations and stored centrally, enabling insights without extra user effort.
Core Principle
Maintain one clean data pipeline for all records (repairs, payments, listings, provider jobs, tenant searches, landlord actions). Insights are delivered through dashboards and reports tailored to each audience.
Data Capture Scope
 Repairs & Maintenance: type, frequency, cost, provider, specific brand used, and replacement lifecycle.
 Tenants: demographics, profession, rental history, preferences, payment behavior.
 Landlords: ownership type (diaspora, estate developer, individual, corporate) and portfolio metrics.
 Properties: design features, upgrades, occupancy, risk classifications (e.g., GIS flood zones).
 Providers: completion times, warranty use, material quality, client ratings.
 Listings & Market Activity: search filters, inquiries, engagement with photos/videos, pricing outcomes.
Data Reuse by Angle
 Internal Operations: predict risk-prone properties, detect weak providers, forecast revenue.
 Landlords: portfolio benchmarking, pricing advice, durability guidance (brands/materials).
 Tenants: property matching, risk alerts, transparent service quality.
 Service Providers: ranking, demand insights, accountability for materials used.
 Secure Living Growth: new product modules, hardware partnerships, county expansion.
 External Market: anonymized rental trends, design demand, material durability reports.
System Note
 Data collection must be transparent and anonymized where required.
 Sensitive fields (e.g., tenant professions) require explicit opt-in.
 Dashboards should automatically highlight insights, ensuring data intelligence serves both operations and advisory roles without added user effort.


 Implementation Priorities (ordered list for sprint planning)
 Enforce role-based access and mandatory dropdowns for inspections, repairs, meter readings.
 Supervisor Dashboard: meter entry, inspection checklist, verification workflow, photo upload.
 Event Store implementation (capture every important action as an event).
 Trust account & milestone escrow logic with gating rules for payment release.
 Tenant App: complaints, payments, feedback, referrals.
 Professional App: task acceptance, evidence upload, brand/component selection.
 Admin Panel: central taxonomies and "Other" review queue.
 Data Warehouse & ETL pipelines for dashboards and ML.
 Geospatial services: GPS capture, survey upload, flood risk tagging.
 Proxy/consignment workflows and legal POA onboarding.
 Integrations: M-Pesa, Lands Registry, CRB, mapping & weather APIs.
 Data quality automation: validation rules, anomaly detection, admin alerts.
 Staff enablement: training modules, quick-win widgets, gamification.
 Backups, replication, RTO/RPO verification & recovery drills.

Monitoring, KPIs & Success Metrics
 Data completeness per property (target > 90%).
 % of verified meter readings vs total readings (target > 85%).
 Average time to close work order (SLA).
 Tenant NPS and referral conversion rate.
 False positive anomaly alert rate (keep low to avoid alert fatigue).
 Staff data quality score (accuracy & completeness).
 Uptake of paid data products (subscriptions sold).

Legal, Compliance & Diaspora Safeguards (must-haves)
 POA requirement  --  any proxy action requiring legal representation (property collection, registration filings) requires an uploaded and verified Power of Attorney as part of onboarding. The system will gate such services until POA validated.
 Terms of Service & Liability  --  consignment and purchase services must present clear ToS that limits Secure Living's liability for latent title defects discovered after purchase, while describing the extent of due diligence performed.
 Financial Compliance  --  trust and escrow accounts designed to be auditable and compliant with Central Bank of Kenya regulations. All trust flows are logged, reconciled, and exportable for regulatory review.
 Consent & Benchmarking Opt-in  --  clients give explicit consent for their (anonymized) data to be included in benchmarking products; opt-out preserved.

Final Operational Clarifications (to remove ambiguity)
 Caretakers do NOT access the app; they report to Supervisors for meter readings and evidence. Supervisors are the official system user for these inputs.
 Garbage & estate service fees are invoiced separately; they are not part of meter calculation.
 Verification gates: Supervisor sign-off required before final invoice generation, milestone payment release, title pick-up confirmation.
 No free-text for critical taxonomies: free-text only allowed when "Other" is used and then routed into admin review. Analytics only uses structured values.
 Disputes: any tenant dispute triggers a dispute_event that pauses auto-release of related funds (if funds are involved) until resolution.
Managerial Decisions & Performance Tracking
Secure Living's intelligence framework must also track the human side of operations  --  how staff and managers influence outcomes. Decisions, actions, and collaboration are captured as structured events for accountability, learning, and improvement.
  A. Cross-Functional Collaboration Metrics
 Data quality issue resolution time (operations vs. data team).
 Decision support satisfaction (how well data workers serve managers).
  B. Learning & Development Indicators
 Skill progression (taxonomy categories mastered by supervisors and staff).
 Analytics adoption rate (how quickly managers adopt new dashboards/models).
  C. Innovation Contributions
 Process improvement suggestions submitted and implemented.
 Taxonomy or workflow enhancements proposed and adopted.
  D. Balanced Scorecard
 Leading indicators: data completeness, responsiveness to anomalies.
 Lagging indicators: revenue growth, tenant retention, reduced arrears.
  E. Decision Tracking
 Managers log critical decisions (rent adjustment, eviction choice, investment in repairs).
 Each decision tagged with expected outcome --> later matched with actual results.
 Enables longitudinal analysis of decision effectiveness.
  F. Implementation Safeguards
 Peer calibration across managers to ensure fairness.
 Context adjustment for property complexity (high-risk neighborhoods vs. premium estates).
 Psychological safety preserved: failed but documented experiments not penalized.
  
Data Worker Categories, Performance Statistics & Human Accountability 
Secure Living's system not only captures real estate and financial data, but also ensures that human contributors  --  staff, analysts, and decision-makers  --  are accountable, incentivized, and continuously improving. This section defines performance metrics, safeguards, and implementation rules for tracking contributions across all levels.

1. Categories of Data Workers
 Operational Real Estate Data Workers
Roles: supervisors, leasing officers, technicians, customer care.
Focus: frontline data accuracy, timeliness, and service quality.
 Data Intelligence & Governance Workers
Roles: admins, analysts, compliance officers.
Focus: taxonomy management, anomaly resolution, insight delivery, regulatory compliance.
 Managerial & Executive Decision Makers
Roles: property managers, portfolio heads, executives.
Focus: quality of strategic and tactical decisions and their business impact.

2. Core Metrics by Category
Operational Workers
 Data Completeness Score
 Accuracy Rate (validated entries)
 SLA Compliance (complaints, work orders, inspections)
 Tenant/Landlord Feedback Rating
 Dispute Ratio (entries escalated)
Data Workers (Admins/Analysts)
 Taxonomy Responsiveness (time to classify "Other")
 Data Quality Interventions (% anomalies resolved)
 Insight SLA (timeliness of reports/dashboards)
 Predictive Model Accuracy
 Compliance Accuracy (documents, legal validations)
 Revenue Impact from Data Products
Managers/Decision Makers
 Decision-to-Outcome Impact (% decisions producing positive results)
 Data-Driven Decision Ratio (% referencing structured data)
 Financial Impact of Decisions
 Tenant/Client Sentiment After Decisions
 Escalation Avoidance Rate

3. Cross-Functional, Learning & Innovation Metrics
 Collaboration:
 Data Quality Issue Resolution Time (ops + data teams)
 Decision Support Satisfaction (how well analysts serve managers)
